# Verita Frontend — Exhaustive Scan

_18-agent scan: 12 file-by-file deep reads of `frontend/src` (63 files, ~11,750 LOC) + 6 cross-cutting audits (a11y, design-system, performance, type-safety, data-flow, build-health)._

## ⚠️ Headline: the entire `src/immersive/` 3D tree is dead code

Both the design-system and data-flow audits independently confirmed (via grep) that `immersive/ImmersiveLanding.tsx` and its ~25 supporting files (BuildingScene, all floors, DOM overlays, hooks, BuildingContext) are **never imported by any route or component**. The live landing is `pages/Landing.tsx`. Also dead: `sections/Hero.tsx`, `sections/LedgerSections.tsx`, `components/SignalField.tsx`.

**Consequence:** many high-severity issues in the per-file detail (timer/GSAP leaks, three.js material leaks, NDC-ellipse shader, Preloader boot-hang) sit in code that does not currently execute — real if the layer is wired in, inert today. They're tagged `[immersive/dead]` below. The audit also found two *latent data bugs* in that layer that would bite the moment it ships (risk_level case mismatch; quality_score scale).

## Cross-cutting scorecard

| Dimension | Grade | Findings |

| Accessibility | (see summary) | 10 |
| Design-system fidelity to "The Forensic Ledger" | (see summary) | 11 |

| Frontend build / typecheck / test health | D+ — tests genuinely pass but tsc fails (5 errors) and `npm run test` falsely exits 1; coverage ~5% | 4 |

## Cross-cutting audits

### Accessibility (WCAG AAA, per PRODUCT.md)


> PRODUCT.md (lines 76-85) makes a hard, explicit commitment: WCAG AAA contrast (7:1 body / 4.5:1 large), full prefers-reduced-motion alternatives including "the custom cursor must yield to the native one," a keyboard-navigable workspace, and auditing "both worlds." The implementation falls well short of that bar. The contrast system is built on muted tokens (--text-muted) that fail even AA, let alone AAA, and these tokens carry load-bearing UI text everywhere (mono micro-labels, table headers, body copy). The reduced-motion story is partially handled in CSS but has a direct contradiction of the stated promise: cursor:none is applied unconditionally and is never restored under prefers-reduced-motion, so reduced-motion users lose their pointer entirely. Forms across Settings/SqlPlayground/NLP/Risk have zero programmatic labels (no <label>/htmlFor/aria-label on the inputs themselves) and inputs strip their focus outline with no replacement. There is no skip link, no :focus-visible styling anywhere, and the heavy ECharts/3D canvases expose no text alternative. The app does use semantic headings, nav, main, and lists in places — a real foundation — but the AAA claim is aspirational rather than met. Note: the immersive 3D building (frontend/src/immersive/*) is fully built but ImmersiveLanding is never imported/routed; the live landing is frontend/src/pages/Landing.tsx. The immersive code is still graded since it is shipped source and a migration candidate, but its severity is discounted as currently-dead.

#### [CRITICAL] --text-muted fails even AA contrast, yet carries load-bearing text everywhere — AAA claim unmet
`frontend/src/index.css` · `frontend/src/components/AppShell.tsx` · `frontend/src/pages/Risk.tsx` · `frontend/src/components/studio/SqlPlayground.tsx`

--text-muted is #756b58 on the dark app (index.css:34). Against --bg-2 #1b1916 (sidebar/header/panels) the ratio is ~3.1:1, and against panel surfaces it is similar. AAA requires 7:1 for body text and 4.5:1 for large; this fails AA (4.5:1) too. This token is not used only for decoration — it carries real, often body-sized information: nav item labels for inactive routes (AppShell.tsx:185), the 'CASE FILE /' breadcrumb and section labels, every mono micro-label/labelStyle across Risk/NLP/Settings/Overview, table header rows (Risk.tsx:193, SqlPlayground.tsx:127), helper text ('Ctrl+Enter to run…' SqlPlayground.tsx:103), and the input placeholder (SqlPlayground.tsx:73). The light theme's --text-muted #4a4434 on #f3eee2 is ~6.3:1 — better, still under the 7:1 body bar. PRODUCT.md:78-80 explicitly says muted-text tokens must hold the AAA bar in both worlds; they do not.

**Fix:** Darken the surfaces or lighten the muted tokens until 7:1 holds for body and 4.5:1 for large in BOTH themes. Practically: raise dark --text-muted toward ~#a99e86+ and verify each surface pairing, or reserve the current value strictly for ≥18.66px-bold large text and introduce a separate AAA-passing token for the pervasive small mono labels. Add an automated contrast test over the token matrix so regressions are caught.

#### [CRITICAL] cursor:none is never restored under prefers-reduced-motion — direct violation of stated promise
`frontend/src/index.css` · `frontend/src/components/CustomCursor.tsx` · `frontend/src/immersive/dom/UVCursor.tsx`

index.css:84-87 applies `body { cursor: none }` and `a,button,[data-cursor]{cursor:none}` for any fine pointer, unconditionally. The reduced-motion block (index.css:717-728) only disables animations/transitions; it never re-enables the cursor. The JS cursors correctly bail under reduced motion (CustomCursor.tsx:35 returns null, UVCursor.tsx:57 returns null), so a reduced-motion user gets NO custom cursor AND NO native cursor — the pointer becomes invisible across the entire app and landing. PRODUCT.md:81-83 explicitly requires 'the custom cursor must yield to the native one.' This is also a WCAG operability problem independent of the brand promise.

**Fix:** Inside @media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) add `body, a, button, [data-cursor] { cursor: auto !important; }`. Also restore the native cursor on coarse/touch pointers explicitly. Ideally gate the cursor:none rule behind `@media (pointer:fine) and (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)`.

#### [HIGH] Form controls have no programmatic labels (no <label>, htmlFor, or aria-label on inputs)
`frontend/src/components/studio/SqlPlayground.tsx` · `frontend/src/pages/NLP.tsx` · `frontend/src/pages/Risk.tsx`

No <label> or htmlFor exists anywhere in the codebase. The SQL NL search input (SqlPlayground.tsx:69-75) relies on a placeholder only. The SQL editor <textarea> (SqlPlayground.tsx:91) has no label — the nearby 'table: data' text is not associated. The NLP <textarea> (NLP.tsx:137) is preceded by a styled div 'COMPLIANCE TEXT' that is not a label and not linked. The Risk decision-threshold <input type=range> (Risk.tsx:152-160) has no label/aria-label/aria-valuetext, so a screen reader announces only 'slider' with a raw number. Placeholders are not accessible names (WCAG 3.3.2 / 1.3.1, 4.1.2).

**Fix:** Add a visible <label htmlFor> for each control (the existing 'COMPLIANCE TEXT'/'DECISION THRESHOLD' labels can become real <label>s), or at minimum aria-label on each input. For the range slider add aria-label='Decision threshold' and aria-valuetext={threshold.toFixed(2)}.

#### [HIGH] No visible focus indicator: inputs set outline:none and there is no :focus-visible styling anywhere
`frontend/src/index.css` · `frontend/src/components/studio/SqlPlayground.tsx` · `frontend/src/pages/NLP.tsx` · `frontend/src/components/AppShell.tsx` · `frontend/src/pages/Settings.tsx`

The SQL inputs and NLP textarea explicitly set outline:'none' (SqlPlayground.tsx:74,100; NLP.tsx:152) with no replacement. There is no :focus or :focus-visible rule in index.css at all, and all interactive styling in the app is inline with hover-only handlers (onMouseEnter/onMouseLeave) — keyboard focus produces no visible change on nav links, theme toggle, buttons, or the external links in Settings. This fails WCAG 2.4.7 (and AAA 2.4.11/2.4.13). Keyboard users cannot tell where focus is.

**Fix:** Add a global :focus-visible rule (e.g. `:focus-visible{outline:2px solid var(--foil-gold);outline-offset:2px}`) and remove the bare outline:none from inputs (or pair it with a focus box-shadow). Ensure focus state is at least as prominent as the hover state, and meets the 3:1 focus-indicator contrast bar.

#### [HIGH] Charts and 3D canvases expose no text alternative or accessible name
`frontend/src/pages/Risk.tsx` · `frontend/src/components/EChart.tsx` · `frontend/src/pages/Landing.tsx` · `frontend/src/immersive/canvas/BuildingScene.tsx`

ECharts canvases (Risk.tsx ROC/PR/feature-importance via EChart.tsx, Overview/Studio charts) render to <canvas> with no role, aria-label, or adjacent text summary of the data, so the actual numbers are unavailable to screen readers (the surrounding KPI cards help but the curves/importance bars do not). The landing FraudNetwork <canvas> (Landing.tsx:673) and the entire immersive BuildingScene <Canvas> (BuildingScene.tsx:201) have no aria-hidden, no role='img', no fallback children, and no description. WCAG 1.1.1.

**Fix:** Give each meaningful chart container role='img' with an aria-label summarizing the key figure (e.g. 'ROC curve, AUC 0.998'), or render a visually-hidden data table. Mark purely decorative canvases (FraudNetwork, the building scene) aria-hidden='true' and ensure all their information is conveyed in adjacent text.

#### [HIGH] No skip link and landing nav is hover-color-only
`frontend/src/App.tsx` · `frontend/src/components/AppShell.tsx` · `frontend/src/pages/Landing.tsx`

App.tsx/AppShell.tsx provide no 'skip to content' link, so keyboard/SR users traverse the full sidebar nav on every route (WCAG 2.4.1, AAA-relevant). The landing Nav module links (Landing.tsx:99-107) and hero buttons communicate interactivity and state purely through onMouseEnter/onMouseLeave color swaps with no focus equivalent, compounding the focus-visibility gap for keyboard users on the brand showpiece.

**Fix:** Add a visually-hidden-until-focused skip link as the first focusable element targeting <main>. Replace hover-only affordances with state that also responds to :focus-visible.

#### [MEDIUM] Color tokens for danger/violet/blue chart accents and tier dots are below AAA on dark surfaces
`frontend/src/index.css` · `frontend/src/pages/Risk.tsx` · `frontend/src/pages/NLP.tsx` · `frontend/src/pages/Settings.tsx`

Semantic accents kept 'at original values for chart/alert compat' (index.css:36-43) are used as text and as the only differentiator for status. E.g. --violet #a855f7 as text on dark surface (Settings.tsx:165, Risk data-source banner), tier names colored by TIER_COLOR with #f5a524/#ff7a8a (Risk.tsx:21-23), and the NLP risk-level color used as large numerals (NLP.tsx:205). Several pass AA-large but not the 7:1 body bar; the small mono framework pills (NLP.tsx:259-265) at ~0.62rem on tinted backgrounds are likely under 4.5:1. Status is also conveyed by color alone (the 7px tier dots in Risk.tsx:203 and Settings.tsx:148 health dots) — WCAG 1.4.1.

**Fix:** Audit each accent-on-surface pairing for the size it is actually used at; reserve thin/small accent text for AAA-passing shades. Pair every status color with a non-color cue (icon, shape, or text label) — the tier name text next to the dot already helps, but the Settings health dot has no text state.

#### [MEDIUM] Immersive 3D experience is entirely keyboard- and SR-inaccessible (shipped but currently unrouted)
`frontend/src/immersive/ImmersiveLanding.tsx` · `frontend/src/immersive/canvas/BuildingScene.tsx` · `frontend/src/immersive/dom/DossierNav.tsx` · `frontend/src/immersive/dom/ElevatorHUD.tsx`

ImmersiveLanding (frontend/src/immersive/ImmersiveLanding.tsx) is a full WebGL building with navigation living only in pointer-driven overlays. DossierNav and ElevatorHUD use real <button>s (good — keyboard reachable) but the whole journey (preloader gate, stamp intro, camera teleports, UV reveal) has no SR description, no live-region announcements of floor changes, no escape/skip, and forces cursor:none (ImmersiveLanding.tsx:36). There is no non-3D fallback path to the same content. It is exported but never imported (only Landing.tsx is routed at '/'), so user impact is currently nil — but it is shipped source and a documented migration candidate.

**Fix:** Before routing it live, add: a reduced-motion/SR fallback that links to the same /studio,/risk,/nlp,/overview destinations as plain content; aria-live announcements on floor/phase changes; an escape-to-skip affordance; and remove the unconditional cursor:none under reduced motion. If it stays dead, consider deleting it to avoid shipping inaccessible code.

#### [MEDIUM] Decorative/ambient animations not all covered by reduced-motion; some run from inline styles GSAP/framer can't be killed by CSS
`frontend/src/pages/Landing.tsx` · `frontend/src/immersive/dom/ElevatorHUD.tsx` · `frontend/src/components/studio/SqlPlayground.tsx` · `frontend/src/components/AppShell.tsx`

The CSS reduced-motion block (index.css:717-728) only neutralizes CSS animations/transitions. GSAP timelines and ScrollTrigger pins in Landing.tsx (e.g. ImpactText pin scrub 530-541, HorizontalStats pinned horizontal scroll 567-576, hero scroll fade 146-149) and framer-motion entrance/loop animations (DossierNav, ElevatorHUD TravelIndicator infinite pulse 128-131, SqlPlayground result motion.div) are driven in JS and are NOT gated on prefers-reduced-motion, so they keep running for reduced-motion users. The live hash tickers (AppShell.tsx:23-32 setInterval, also Landing fakeHash) also animate content continuously with no reduced-motion or pause control (WCAG 2.2.2).

**Fix:** Guard GSAP/framer-motion setups with a window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)') check (jump to final state / disable pins and scrubs), as already done in Hero.tsx and the counters. Provide a pause for the continuously updating hash tickers or render them static under reduced motion.

#### [LOW] Heading hierarchy and landmark labelling gaps
`frontend/src/components/AppShell.tsx` · `frontend/src/components/studio/Investigator.tsx`

AppShell <nav> (line 168) and the sidebar <aside> have no aria-label, and there are two navs (sidebar + logo link region) without distinguishing names. The Risk/NLP/Settings pages use a styled <div> 'CASE FILE / …' eyebrow above the <h1> which is fine, but several large numeric stats are <div>s with no semantic role (acceptable). Investigator.tsx:63 and ErrorBoundary.tsx:25 use <h2> with no preceding <h1> in their isolated render context. Minor, but tidy for AAA-level rigor.

**Fix:** Add aria-label to the primary <nav> ('Primary'), and verify each route renders exactly one <h1> with descending levels. Low priority relative to contrast/focus/labels.

**Done well:**
- Real prefers-reduced-motion coverage exists: index.css:717-728 kills animations/transitions and forces evidence cards visible; CustomCursor (CustomCursor.tsx:21), UVCursor (UVCursor.tsx:57), Hero typewriter (sections/Hero.tsx:97), and LedgerSections counters (105,138) all branch on reduced-motion to skip or jump to final state.
- Semantic HTML foundation is present: <nav>/<aside>/<header>/<main> in AppShell.tsx, a single <h1> per page (Risk/NLP/Settings/Overview/Studio), real <ul>/<li> for NLP signals, and <table>/<thead>/<th> for tabular data (Risk.tsx, SqlPlayground.tsx).
- Some ARIA done right: Studio.tsx:359,372 use aria-label on icon-only buttons; LedgerSections marks decorative elements aria-hidden (86,167-168,197,234,239,305) and aria-pressed on the UV toggle (221); decorative tape/barcode/fingerprint correctly hidden from SR.
- NLP entity highlighting uses the semantic <mark> element (NLP.tsx:49).
- Theme persists via localStorage and applies a light 'Porcelain Ledger' palette (themeStore.ts) giving users a higher-contrast option.

---

### Design-system fidelity to "The Forensic Ledger" (DESIGN.md / PRODUCT.md)


> Verita's design system is exceptionally well-documented and the canonical surfaces are real, not aspirational: index.css carries a complete ledger token set (paper/ink/stamp/foil, hard offset shadows, square corners, mono micro-labels) and the live landing (pages/Landing.tsx) lands the Forensic Ledger identity convincingly in its lower half (HowItWorks, LedgerCTA — torn edges, stamps, wax seal, sealing chain, guilloche, hash ticker). The named rules are genuinely encoded in CSS (.stamp, .chain-node.sealed, .paper-terminal, .ledger-label, .hard-shadow values). However, fidelity drops sharply in three areas. (1) The flagship "signature component" — the Investigator hash-chain trace — is the single most legacy-aurora surface in the app: full glassmorphism, aurora gradient (#6366f1→#a855f7), blur-glow elevation (direct Hard Shadow Rule violation), rounded corners, and the literally-forbidden #8c97b5 muted gray. PRODUCT.md says tamper-evidence "should be visible and felt... wherever trust is the message," yet the canonical trust surface looks like the anti-reference. (2) The workspace pages (Risk, NLP, Investigator) define ad-hoc severity/entity color maps in raw hex (#ff2d55, #ff7a8a, #f5a524) that aren't tokens at all, plus lean on dark-world-only signals (--blue/--violet/--cyan) on surfaces that also render in light/Porcelain mode — where DESIGN.md's Iron-Gall Rule forbids anything but ink and Faded Ink. (3) The landing's top half (HeroSection + module showcase) introduces an entire amber machine-world palette (#ffb700 AMBER) that exists in neither the DESIGN.md color list nor as a token, and the whole file hardcodes ~24 hex constants instead of consuming tokens. Separately, a large amount of dead code (sections/Hero.tsx, sections/LedgerSections.tsx, the entire immersive/ tree, SignalField.tsx) ships in the bundle and muddies any audit of "the system." Net: the canon is strong and the ledger DNA is real where it appears, but the system is applied inconsistently and the most important component betrays it.

#### [HIGH] Flagship hash-chain Investigator is pure legacy aurora — the anti-reference applied to the canonical trust surface
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/studio/Investigator.tsx`

DESIGN.md names the Sealing Chain / hash-chain as the Signature Component and PRODUCT.md says tamper-evidence must be 'visible and felt... wherever trust is the message.' Yet Investigator.tsx — the autonomous-agent reasoning trace, the product's thesis made UI — is the most legacy-glass component in the app. It uses className='glass' on every panel; an aurora gradient icon/button background 'linear-gradient(135deg,#6366f1,#a855f7)' (line 60) and 'linear-gradient(120deg,#6366f1,#a855f7)' (line 75); blur-glow elevation 'box-shadow: 0 10px 40px -8px rgba(168,85,247,0.6)' (lines 60, 75) — a direct violation of the Hard Shadow Rule ('if a shadow is blurry and it isn't a photograph, it's wrong'); rounded corners everywhere (borderRadius 18, 12, 10, 8, 999 — lines 60,75,99,112,156,183,186) against the 'square is certain' rule; legacy signals var(--blue) for the chain hashes (lines 71,112,166,182) instead of Registrar Green (the spec literally says 'the live hash ticker (Registrar Green mono)'); and the explicitly forbidden gray '#8c97b5' hardcoded at line 140. None of the canonical chain-node/sealed/stamp/paper-terminal vocabulary is used here. The chain is supposed to flip ruled-gray nodes to gold-foil with the sealed glow; instead it's flat aurora cards.

**Fix:** Rebuild Investigator on ledger DNA: replace .glass with paper-terminal / 2px-ink-border + 6px 6px 0 desk shadow, square corners, swap the aurora gradients for ink/vermilion, render the reasoning timeline using the real .chain-node/.sealed nodes and the Registrar-Green hash ticker, replace var(--blue) hash text with var(--seal-green), and delete the #8c97b5 literal. This is the highest-leverage fix — it's the demo's centerpiece.

#### [HIGH] Untokenized ad-hoc hex color maps for severity/entity/framework across app pages
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Risk.tsx` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/NLP.tsx` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/studio/Investigator.tsx`

Risk.tsx (line 22), NLP.tsx (lines 13-22), and Investigator.tsx (line 30) each define their own severity/entity/framework color maps using raw hex that exists in NEITHER index.css tokens NOR the DESIGN.md palette: '#ff2d55', '#ff7a8a', '#f5a524', '#16c784'. These are arbitrary one-off pinks/oranges, not the canonical Alarm Red (#ff4d5e / --danger) or semantic tokens. The result is three different 'critical red' values in the app, none matching --stamp-red or --danger, undermining the Ceremony Rule (accents must read as the system's stamps, not random paint). NLP additionally hardcodes the same set and mixes in dark-only --blue/--violet/--cyan tokens for entity highlighting.

**Fix:** Replace these maps with tokens: critical→var(--danger)/var(--stamp-red), medium→var(--foil-gold), low→var(--seal-green). If a richer tier ramp is genuinely needed, define it once as CSS custom properties in index.css (e.g. --tier-critical/--tier-high) that resolve correctly in both dark and light themes, and reference those — never inline hex.

#### [HIGH] Dark-world-only signal colors used on surfaces that also render in light/Porcelain mode — Iron-Gall Rule risk
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/NLP.tsx` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Risk.tsx` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/studio/InsightsPanel.tsx` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Settings.tsx`

DESIGN.md is explicit: --blue/--violet/--cyan and #8c97b5-style grays are 'dark-world tokens'; on paper the only secondary text is Faded Ink (#4a4434), and semantic color should be Vermilion/Registrar Green. But these legacy signals are wired into app surfaces that toggle into light/Porcelain mode (theme can be light): NLP ENTITY_COLOR uses --blue/--violet/--cyan (lines 18-19); Risk chart series hardcode '#4d7cff' and '#a855f7' (lines 286,298) and the importance bar uses gold (good) but ROC/PR lines stay machine-world blue/violet; InsightsPanel uses var(--blue) for the executive-summary accent + icon (lines 27,30) and a hardcoded '#12b5a3' teal fallback (line 49); Settings surfaces var(--violet) for GenAI (lines 40,165). In light mode these don't remap (only --danger/--success are overridden in html.light), so blue/violet/cyan render on cream — exactly what the rule forbids.

**Fix:** Audit every var(--blue)/--violet/--cyan and raw signal hex on app surfaces for light-mode behavior. Either (a) add light-mode overrides in html.light that retint these toward ink/foil/registrar-green, or (b) replace them with ledger accents (foil-gold for neutral emphasis, registrar-green for confirm, vermilion for alarm). Charts should pull series colors from theme-aware tokens, not literals.

#### [MEDIUM] Landing top-half invents an off-spec amber machine-world palette (#ffb700) not in the design system
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Landing.tsx`

pages/Landing.tsx (the LIVE landing at /) hardcodes a full local palette at lines 22-29 (DARK '#0c0a07', AMBER '#ffb700', etc.) and builds the entire HeroSection + Studio module showcase around AMBER as the primary accent (scanner score, accent bars, CTA buttons, watermarks). #ffb700 'amber' appears in index.css only as the legacy Machine-World primitive (--amber) and is NOT in DESIGN.md's color list at all — the ledger's ceremonial accents are vermilion/registrar-green/gold-foil. So the brand showpiece leads with a color the design system doesn't sanction. The amber CRT/scanner aesthetic reads as a third, undocumented world sitting between the canonical ledger and the documented legacy aurora. The lower half of the same file correctly uses tokens (var(--ink)/var(--paper)/var(--stamp-red)), making the inconsistency intra-file.

**Fix:** Decide whether the dark hero is 'legacy machine world, maintained' (then it should use the documented legacy tokens --machine-ground / the aurora signals, not a new amber) or migrate it to ledger DNA (gold-foil instead of amber, ink/paper grounds). Either way, promote the local constants to tokens or to the documented legacy set so the landing stops introducing #ffb700 as a primary brand color.

#### [MEDIUM] Hero gradient text + .gradient-text + var(--signature) appear in dead landing code, and gradient-text is referenced live
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/sections/Hero.tsx` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/index.css`

DESIGN.md/PRODUCT.md name gradient text as part of the anti-reference and say '.gradient-text is legacy; never apply it to new elements.' sections/Hero.tsx still applies className='gradient-text' to the headline (line 230) and uses var(--signature) aurora gradient + blur-glow box-shadow (lines 31,33). This file is dead code (not imported), but it ships and is the kind of pattern that gets copy-pasted. The .gradient-text and --signature definitions remain live in index.css and are easy to reach for.

**Fix:** Delete the dead Hero.tsx/LedgerSections.tsx (see separate dead-code finding). Consider annotating .gradient-text/--signature in index.css with a 'LEGACY — do not apply to new elements' comment so future work doesn't adopt them.

#### [MEDIUM] Custom cursors use legacy aurora signal colors with blur-glow, in both worlds
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/CustomCursor.tsx`

CustomCursor.tsx (app shell, all routes incl. light mode) renders the ring in '#4d7cff' with 'box-shadow: 0 0 24px 2px rgba(77,124,255,0.5)' and the dot in '#22d3ee' with a cyan glow (lines 61-62, 82-83). The landing's own Cursor (Landing.tsx lines 67-71) uses AMBER. Neither uses ledger ink/foil. On the Porcelain (light) app surface a glowing electric-blue cursor is squarely the dark-world aesthetic on paper. mix-blend screen + colored glow is exactly the luminous-aurora texture being migrated away from.

**Fix:** Retint the app cursor to ink/foil (e.g. ring in var(--ink) or var(--foil-gold), no colored glow — a hard 1.5px ring), and make it theme-aware. This is low-effort and visible on every app screen.

#### [MEDIUM] Dark-app shadows hardcode rgba(0,0,0,...) instead of the ink-based desk-object shadow
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Overview.tsx` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Risk.tsx` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/NLP.tsx` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Settings.tsx` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/index.css`

DESIGN.md specifies hard offset shadows in ink: '6px 6px 0 rgba(20,18,11,0.22)'. Across the app, panels use 'box-shadow: 4px 4px 0 rgba(0,0,0,0.35)' (Overview line 34, Risk lines 28/242, NLP line 29, Settings line 16, Studio multiple) and 3px/2px black variants on buttons. Pure black (0,0,0) instead of iron-gall ink (20,18,11) is a subtle but systemic deviation from the documented shadow vocabulary, and .ledger-panel in index.css already does it the right way for light mode (rgba(20,18,11,0.16)) — so the values are inconsistent between the CSS helper and the inline page styles.

**Fix:** Centralize the desk-object shadow as a token/util (e.g. --shadow-desk: 6px 6px 0 rgba(20,18,11,0.22) with a dark-mode variant) and have all panels/buttons consume it instead of inlining rgba(0,0,0,...). This also fixes the magnitude drift (4px vs the documented 6px).

#### [MEDIUM] App page headings use Fraunces serif where DESIGN.md assigns Space Grotesk (Title) to workspace headings
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Overview.tsx` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Risk.tsx` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/NLP.tsx` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Settings.tsx`

DESIGN.md typography hierarchy: 'Title (Space Grotesk 700): App page headings (Studio, Overview, Settings)'; Fraunces Headline is for 'Ledger section headings,' and the One Exhibit Rule reserves Display for the landing. But every app page h1 uses var(--font-serif) Fraunces 900: Overview line 86, Risk line 100, NLP line 98, Settings line 53, Studio line 278. Meanwhile the sidebar logo and chart-panel titles use var(--font-display) (Space Grotesk). So the documented Title role (Space Grotesk for page headings) is inverted with the Headline role. It reads fine, but it's a direct deviation from the named hierarchy and blurs the landing-vs-app typographic distinction the system intends.

**Fix:** Either update the app page h1s to var(--font-display) Space Grotesk per the spec, or update DESIGN.md if the team has deliberately decided app headings should be Fraunces. Pick one and make it consistent; right now code and doc disagree.

#### [NIT] Radius tokens exceed the ledger's 'square + 6px stamp' vocabulary and are widely used
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/index.css` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/studio/InsightsPanel.tsx`

DESIGN.md: 'Don't round corners on ledger components. Square is certain; 14-28px radii are legacy glass,' and rounded.stamp is the only non-zero ledger radius (6px). index.css defines --radius-sm 4 / md 8 / lg 12 / xl 18, and .glass applies --radius-lg (12px) — so any component using .glass (InsightsPanel, Investigator, WhatChanged, TimeMachine, etc.) inherits legacy rounding. The token names don't signal 'legacy only,' inviting continued use on new surfaces.

**Fix:** Mark the md/lg/xl radii as legacy-glass-only in index.css comments, and prefer --radius-none (or --radius-sm 4px max) on migrated surfaces. Longer term, retire .glass in favor of .ledger-panel for new app work.

**Done well:**
- index.css is a faithful, near-complete encoding of DESIGN.md: every named ledger primitive exists as a real class — .stamp (3px double, turbulence mask, rotation), .chain-node/.sealed (gold glow exactly matching the 'Sealed node' shadow spec), .paper-terminal (#fffdf6, 2px ink, 6px 6px 0 desk shadow), .wax-seal, .ledger-label, .microtext, .guilloche, .exhibit-frame, UV mode. The shadow values match the DESIGN.md vocabulary verbatim.
- The landing's lower half (Landing.tsx HowItWorks + LedgerCTA, lines 600-809) is a textbook execution of the Forensic Ledger: torn-edge paper, rotated dual stamps (CASE OPEN / COURT READY / VERIFIED / CASE SEALED), animated sealing chain with connectors, wax seal, ink-fill buttons with stamp-red hard shadows (5-6px 6px 0 var(--stamp-red)), all on tokenized var(--ink)/var(--paper).
- The Mono Witness Rule is followed almost everywhere — hashes, IDs, scores, timestamps, KPI values, and thresholds are consistently var(--font-mono) across Overview, Risk, NLP, Studio, and AppShell.
- AppShell sidebar was clearly migrated toward ledger DNA per the brief: square corners (borderRadius: 0), vermilion stamp-dot active marker (borderLeft 2px stamp-red), mono CASE FILE labels, an animated integrity chain + live hash ticker at the spine bottom, and a stamp-ring avatar — exactly the 'when rebuilt' guidance in DESIGN.md section 5.
- prefers-reduced-motion is handled responsibly: the global media query in index.css kills animations, and CustomCursor + Hero Typewriter check matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)') and bail to instant fallbacks.
- Accessibility intent is real: tokens are AAA-tuned (ink #14120b ~15:1, faded ink #4a4434 ~7.7:1), light/Porcelain mode remaps --danger/--success to vermilion/registrar-green per the 'on paper use Vermilion and Registrar Green' rule, and buttons carry aria-labels in Studio.

### TypeScript Type Safety & Code Quality


> The Verita frontend is broadly disciplined: zero stray console.* (only one intentional, eslint-suppressed console.error in ErrorBoundary), zero TODO/FIXME/HACK markers, zero @ts-ignore/@ts-nocheck escape hatches, and no empty catch blocks. tsconfig runs strict:true. However, the codebase does NOT currently type-check: `npx tsc --noEmit` reports 5 real errors in pages/Overview.tsx, meaning broken types are committed on main. Beyond that, type safety is undermined in the data-handling layer (pages + studio components) by a consistent reliance on `any`: ~30 `: any` annotations, 8 `catch (e: any)` blocks, untyped fetch responses, and a defensive `as any` cast in Settings. Non-null assertions (`!`) are used pragmatically (R3F refs are idiomatic) but a few in render paths rely on implicit phase invariants. The Three.js/immersive layer is well-typed (proper `as THREE.XxxMaterial` casts, typed refs). Quality issues are minor: duplicated theme-color literals, one stale-closure forecast cache, and inconsistent r.ok checking across fetch sites. No dead code or commented-out blocks of significance found.</summary>
<parameter name="score">B- — clean hygiene (no console/TODO/ts-ignore) but the committed code fails tsc, and the data layer leans on `any` instead of typed API contracts

#### [HIGH] Committed code does not type-check — 5 tsc errors in Overview.tsx
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Overview.tsx:56` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Overview.tsx:58` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Overview.tsx:59` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Overview.tsx:60`

`npx tsc --noEmit` exits non-zero with 5 errors, all in pages/Overview.tsx (lines 58-60). The fetch helper `const j = (p) => fetch(...).then(r => (r.ok ? r.json() : {})).catch(() => ({}))` infers a return type of `Promise<{} | {}>` (the empty-object literal). Downstream accesses `s.analyses`, `s.investigations`, `s.queries`, `a.analyses`, `i.investigations` then fail TS2339 (Property does not exist on type '{}'). Despite strict:true, this builds via Vite (esbuild does not type-check) so it ships, but `tsc` / CI / IDE all flag it. This means strict mode is effectively not being enforced in the dev loop.

**Fix:** Type the helper's return explicitly, e.g. `const j = <T,>(p: string): Promise<Partial<T>> => fetch(...).then(r => (r.ok ? r.json() : {} as Partial<T>)).catch(() => ({}))`, or define a `SummaryResponse`/`HistoryResponse` interface and annotate the destructured results. Add `tsc --noEmit` to the lint/CI step so this can't regress.

#### [MEDIUM] Pervasive `catch (e: any)` defeats strict error handling
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Studio.tsx:188` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/NLP.tsx:84` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/studio/Investigator.tsx:50` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/studio/SqlPlayground.tsx:38` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/studio/SqlPlayground.tsx:57` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/studio/WhatChanged.tsx:29`

8 catch clauses annotate the error as `any` and then read `e.message` directly: Studio.tsx:188-189, NLP.tsx:84, Investigator.tsx:50-51, SqlPlayground.tsx:38 & 57, plus WhatChanged.tsx:29 (`.catch((e) => setError(e.message))`, e implicitly any). In strict TS the caught binding should be `unknown` and narrowed. Several have no fallback: Investigator.tsx:51 (`setError(e.message)`), SqlPlayground.tsx:39/58, and WhatChanged.tsx:29 will surface `undefined` (rendered as the string "undefined") if a non-Error value is thrown or `message` is absent. Studio.tsx:189 and NLP.tsx do guard with `|| "..."`.

**Fix:** Change to `catch (e: unknown)` and extract via a tiny helper: `const msg = e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)`. Apply uniformly so every error path has a guaranteed string fallback.

#### [MEDIUM] API response shapes are untyped `any` instead of declared interfaces
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Studio.tsx:24` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Studio.tsx:41` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/studio/Investigator.tsx:11` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/studio/ProfileRail.tsx:24` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/studio/SqlPlayground.tsx:8`

Core domain objects flow through the app as `any`. Studio.tsx's `Result` interface declares `profile: any`, `dashboard: any[]`, `quality: any`, `relationships: { nodes: any[]; edges: any[] }` (lines 41-46), and `buildLayout(charts: any[])` (24), `pinned: useState<any[]>` (122). Investigator.tsx Step.result is `any[]` (11). ProfileRail takes `profile: any` and maps `(col: any)` (24, 83). SqlPlayground rows are `Record<string, any>[]` (8). This means downstream accesses like `data.profile.row_count`, `data.quality.score`, `data.relationships.edges.length` (Studio.tsx:177-181) and `r.profile.row_count` (Studio.tsx:284, Investigator render) get NO type checking — a backend field rename would fail silently at runtime, not compile time. useFloorData.ts shows the right pattern (inline-typed `d.analyses` shapes) and could be promoted to shared interfaces.

**Fix:** Define a shared `types/api.ts` with Profile, Quality, ChartSpec, Relationship, Insight, DashboardResult, InvestigationStep interfaces (the backend Pydantic models are the source of truth) and replace the `any`s. This eliminates most other findings at once.

#### [MEDIUM] Fetch responses set into state without r.ok checks (Risk & Overview)
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Risk.tsx:58` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/immersive/hooks/useFloorData.ts:22` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/immersive/hooks/useLiveLedger.ts:43`

Risk.tsx:58-62 does `Promise.all([fetch(metrics).then(r=>r.json()), fetch(alerts).then(r=>r.json())])` and immediately `setMetrics(m); setAlerts(a.alerts || [])` with NO `r.ok` check. If the API returns a 4xx/5xx with a JSON error body (e.g. `{detail: ...}`), that error object is stored as `metrics` and rendered as if valid; only a network-level throw or non-JSON body hits the catch. Same gap in Overview.tsx:56 (mitigated there by `r.ok ? r.json() : {}`) and the immersive hooks useFloorData.ts / useLiveLedger.ts (lines 87-89, 116-118, 43-45) which call `.then(r => r.json())` with no ok-check — they rely on the outer catch, which works but is inconsistent with the explicit `if (!res.ok) throw` pattern used in Studio/SqlPlayground/Investigator.

**Fix:** Standardize on a typed `apiGet<T>(path): Promise<T>` wrapper that checks `res.ok`, parses, and throws a typed error. Replace the ad-hoc `.then(r => r.json())` chains so a 500 can never masquerade as data.

#### [LOW] `as any` cast hides a heterogeneous array shape in Settings
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Settings.tsx:137` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Settings.tsx:37`

Settings.tsx:137 reads `(r as any).accent || "var(--text)"`. The cast exists because the `rows` array (lines 37-43) is built from object literals where only ONE element carries an `accent` property (line 40: GenAI mode). TS infers a union where `accent` is not on every member, so the access is cast away rather than typed. This silences the very check strict mode is meant to provide.

**Fix:** Give the array an explicit element type: `const rows: { icon: LucideIcon; label: string; value: string; ok: boolean; accent?: string }[] = ...`. Then `r.accent` is legal and type-checked, and the cast disappears.

#### [LOW] Non-null assertions on render-path values rely on implicit invariants
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Studio.tsx:271` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Studio.tsx:351` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/studio/Investigator.tsx:86`

`const r = result!` appears in Studio.tsx:271 and Investigator.tsx:86, and `forecast.data!` is dereferenced repeatedly in Studio.tsx:351-378. These are safe today because they sit after early-return phase guards (e.g. `if (phase !== 'ready') return ...`), so `result`/`forecast.data` are guaranteed non-null. But the guarantee is positional, not type-enforced — reordering the early returns or adding a phase would silently break it. main.tsx:6 `document.getElementById("root")!`, Hero.tsx:39 `ref.current!`, and LedgerSections.tsx:173 `imgWrap.current!` are conventional and acceptable. The R3F `useRef<T>(null!)` pattern (FraudNetwork.tsx:56-57,114; SignalField.tsx:13) is idiomatic and correct.

**Fix:** For the render-path ones, prefer narrowing over assertion — e.g. `if (!result) return null` immediately before use, or destructure inside the guarded block — so the non-null fact is type-derived rather than asserted.

#### [LOW] Untyped ECharts callback params typed as `any`
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/studio/GeoMap.tsx:64` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/studio/RelationshipMap.tsx:26` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/studio/RelationshipMap.tsx:68` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Studio.tsx:51`

ECharts formatter/tooltip callbacks are uniformly `(p: any)`: GeoMap.tsx:64, RelationshipMap.tsx:26, TimeMachine.tsx:61, plus a downstream `as any` on series data in RelationshipMap.tsx:68. This is partly forced by ECharts' loose callback typing, but `p.value`/`p.name` accesses are unchecked. The icon field in Studio.tsx:51 (`icon: any`) is a lucide component and should be `LucideIcon`.

**Fix:** Import ECharts' `CallbackDataParams` (from echarts/types) for formatter params, and type `icon` as `LucideIcon` from lucide-react. Where ECharts types are genuinely intractable, isolate the `any` to the narrowest expression.

#### [LOW] Stale-closure forecast cache can serve the wrong chart's forecast
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Studio.tsx:128`

Studio.tsx:128-143 toggleForecast: when `forecast.data` is already populated, toggling forecast on a DIFFERENT chart (`if (forecast.data) { setForecast(f => ({...f, activeId: chartId})); return; }`) reuses the cached forecast from the first chart instead of refetching for the new dataset/series. Since forecast is fetched per `dataset_id` this is usually the same dataset, but the cache is keyed to neither chartId nor a series, so the displayed forecast may not correspond to the chart it's overlaid on. Logic, not a type issue.

**Fix:** Key the cache by chartId (or clear `forecast.data` when activeId changes to a chart whose series differs) so each chart fetches/overlays its own forecast.

#### [NIT] Duplicated theme-color literals across pages
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Studio.tsx:159` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Risk.tsx:51`

The exact muted/grid color expressions are copy-pasted: `const muted = theme === "dark" ? "#756b58" : "#4a4434"` and `const grid = theme === "dark" ? "rgba(232,224,204,0.08)" : "rgba(20,18,11,0.08)"` appear verbatim in both Studio.tsx:159-160 and Risk.tsx:51-52 (and the literals recur in chart components). Drift risk if one is updated.

**Fix:** Extract a `useChartTheme()` hook (or constants in lib/chartOptions.ts) returning `{ muted, grid }` from the theme store, and consume it everywhere charts are rendered.

#### [NIT] `localStorage` theme read cast without validation
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/store/themeStore.ts:17`

themeStore.ts:17 `(localStorage.getItem("verita-theme") as Theme) || "dark"` casts an arbitrary persisted string to the `Theme` union with no validation. A stale/garbage value (e.g. from a renamed theme) would be accepted as a valid Theme and could break theme-conditional logic. Low impact since values are app-controlled.

**Fix:** Validate before trusting: `const stored = localStorage.getItem("verita-theme"); const initial: Theme = stored === "light" || stored === "dark" ? stored : "dark";`

**Done well:**
- Zero stray console.* statements across 60 files — the only one (ErrorBoundary.tsx:17) is intentional and eslint-suppressed
- Zero TODO/FIXME/HACK/XXX markers and zero @ts-ignore/@ts-expect-error/@ts-nocheck escape hatches in the entire src tree
- No empty catch blocks; no large commented-out code blocks (the 238 `//` lines are genuine explanatory comments)
- Immersive/Three.js layer is correctly typed: material casts use specific types (e.g. `as THREE.MeshStandardMaterial` in RiskLabFloor.tsx:108, DetectiveFloor.tsx:183) and refs use the idiomatic `useRef<THREE.Mesh>(null!)` pattern
- Studio upload path (Studio.tsx:168-171) and several fetch sites correctly check res.ok and surface body.detail before throwing
- useFloorData.ts uses Promise.allSettled with per-source fallbacks and a cancelled-flag guard against setState-after-unmount — solid async hygiene

### Frontend data flow, routing & state architecture


> Verita's frontend is a Vite + React 18 + react-router-dom v7 SPA. main.tsx mounts App (StrictMode). App.tsx defines a flat BrowserRouter route table: "/" renders the scroll-driven marketing page pages/Landing.tsx (no AppShell, no CustomCursor), and five routes nest under one layout route AppShell via Outlet: /studio, /overview, /risk, /nlp, /settings. There is no catch-all/404 route. config.ts resolves the backend base from import.meta.env.VITE_API_URL, defaulting to http://localhost:8000; every fetch composes off this single API_BASE. State is deliberately minimal: one global zustand store (store/themeStore.ts) holding dark/light theme (persisted to localStorage, applied by toggling .light on <html>); all page/feature data is local component state fetched on mount/interaction. App pages are genuinely backend-driven (Studio upload->/api/dashboard/*, Risk /api/risk/*, NLP /api/nlp/analyze, Overview /api/history/*, Settings /api/health, plus SQL/Investigator/TimeMachine/WhatChanged), each with an honest 'backend offline' state. Big structural finding: the entire immersive 3D building subsystem under src/immersive (BuildingContext state machine, useLiveLedger polling hook, useFloorData, useCameraJourney, BuildingScene, all floors, DossierNav/ElevatorHUD overlays) is fully implemented but DEAD CODE — ImmersiveLanding is never imported by any route or component, so none of those hooks, the navigation state machine, or their backend polling ever execute. Within that dead layer there are real data-mapping bugs (risk_level case mismatch, quality_score 0-100 vs 0-1 scaling) that would surface if wired in.</summary>
<score>B — clean, honest, minimally-stated app data flow and routing; marked down for a large dead immersive subsystem (with real latent data bugs) shipping unwired, plus a missing 404 route.</score>
<highlights>["Single source of truth for the API base (config.ts) — every fetch in both the app and the dead immersive layer composes off API_BASE, configurable via VITE_API_URL.", "App pages are honestly backend-driven with explicit offline states (Risk.tsx:64, NLP.tsx:85, Settings.tsx live /api/health reflection, Studio upload pipeline); ErrorBoundary wraps every route element (App.tsx:12-14).", "State is minimal and appropriate: one zustand store for theme only, everything else local fetch-on-mount; debounced refetch on the Risk threshold slider (Risk.tsx:54-70) and a self-measuring ResizeObserver for the RGL grid (Studio.tsx:216-229) show runtime-correctness care.", "BuildingContext is a well-structured phase state machine with thoughtful perf choices (mouseNDC in a ref to avoid per-mousemove re-renders, BuildingContext.tsx:50-59; memoized context value) — strong code that is unfortunately unmounted.", "GeoMap degrades gracefully with an honest fallback when the CDN world map is unavailable (GeoMap.tsx:43-54); WhatChanged/TimeMachine hide quietly when the dataset lacks a usable time axis."]

#### [HIGH] Entire immersive 3D building subsystem is dead code — not wired to any route
`D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\App.tsx` · `D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\immersive\ImmersiveLanding.tsx` · `D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\immersive\context\BuildingContext.tsx` · `D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\immersive\hooks\useLiveLedger.ts` · `D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\immersive\hooks\useFloorData.ts` · `D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\immersive\hooks\useCameraJourney.ts`

App.tsx (lines 5, 23) imports Landing from ./pages/Landing for the / route. The immersive experience — immersive/ImmersiveLanding.tsx, which wraps BuildingProvider + the full 3D BuildingScene, DossierNav, ElevatorHUD, FloorContentOverlay, and the three hooks the task asked about (useLiveLedger, useFloorData, useCameraJourney) — is exported (ImmersiveLanding.tsx:71) but a project-wide search finds zero importers (the only match for ImmersiveLanding is its own export line). Consequently the BuildingContext navigation state machine (preloading->stamp_intro->exterior_approach->exterior_idle->entering/teleporting->lobby->elevator_traveling->floor_*->rooftop), the 4s polling loop in useLiveLedger, the per-floor fetches in useFloorData, and the GSAP camera rig in useCameraJourney never run. ~20 files under src/immersive ship with no entry point. The git log (interactive forensic layer) and DESIGN.md Forensic Ledger/building North Star suggest it was the intended landing, but the static marketing Landing is what renders.

**Fix:** Decide intent: either wire ImmersiveLanding into the / route (demoting pages/Landing), or if the static Landing is canonical, delete/quarantine src/immersive to stop shipping dead bundle weight. Document the decision in DESIGN.md since it conflicts with the stated North Star.

#### [HIGH] risk_level case mismatch makes FLAGGED status unreachable in immersive data hooks
`D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\immersive\hooks\useFloorData.ts` · `D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\immersive\hooks\useLiveLedger.ts` · `D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\pages\Overview.tsx`

Backend stores risk_level in title-case (Low/Medium/High/Critical — investigator.py:302, asserted in test_agent.py:20, column String(12) in models_db.py:73). But useFloorData.fetchRisk (useFloorData.ts:49,57) and useLiveLedger.fetchEntries (useLiveLedger.ts:78) compare against UPPERCASE literals ['CRITICAL','HIGH']. Those comparisons never match real data, so every investigation is mis-classified as VERIFIED/clear (flagged counts read 0) regardless of severity. By contrast the LIVE Overview.tsx correctly keys on title-case (Overview.tsx:19-21), confirming the immersive layer uses the wrong convention. Latent today only because the immersive layer is dead; a correctness bug the moment it is wired in.

**Fix:** Normalize casing at the boundary: compare on inv.risk_level?.toUpperCase() (or switch literals to title-case to match Overview). Apply to useFloorData.ts:49-57 and useLiveLedger.ts:78. Introduce a shared risk-level helper so app and immersive layers can't drift.

#### [MEDIUM] quality_score scale confusion (0-100 vs 0-1) in immersive useFloorData
`D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\immersive\hooks\useFloorData.ts` · `D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\immersive\dom\FloorContentOverlay.tsx`

Backend quality.score is 0-100 (insights.py:222 starts at 100.0, clamped insights.py:248; Overview.tsx renders {score}/100). In useFloorData.fetchStudio: avgQ multiplies again by 100 — Math.round(sum(quality_score*100)/n) (useFloorData.ts:29) — yielding values up to 10000% in the summary string; and the per-item progress bar is fed score: a.quality_score ?? 0.5 (useFloorData.ts:36) where the bar expects 0-1 (FloorContentOverlay DataRow uses right:(1-score)*100%), so a 0-100 value pegs every bar full. The 0.5 default reveals the 0-1 assumption. Dead-code today but a real display bug if shipped.

**Fix:** Pick one scale: treat backend quality_score as 0-100 — avgQ = round(mean(quality_score)); item score = quality_score/100 for the 0-1 bar. Comment the backend's 0-100 contract.

#### [MEDIUM] useLiveLedger is poll-based, not a websocket — and renders convincing placeholder data when backend is offline
`D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\immersive\hooks\useLiveLedger.ts`

Despite the live name and the task's websocket question, useLiveLedger uses HTTP polling: setInterval(poll, 4000) (useLiveLedger.ts:239) hitting /api/history/{analyses,queries,investigations} via Promise.allSettled, plus a requestAnimationFrame loop that only scrolls the canvas (useLiveLedger.ts:225-231). There is no websocket anywhere in the frontend. On fetch failure it silently swaps in placeholder() — 14 fabricated rows with realistic filenames/SQL/hashes (useLiveLedger.ts:93-109) rendered with no visible offline indicator, and the footer always prints a synthetic rotating CHAIN HEAD (tickHash, useLiveLedger.ts:30-36) independent of real data. So an offline backend looks identical to a live, populated ledger. (Dead code currently.) Contrasts with app pages, which show explicit 'Backend unavailable — start the API on :8000' states (Risk.tsx:64, NLP.tsx:85, Settings.tsx:111).

**Fix:** If/when shipped: add a visible SAMPLE/OFFLINE watermark when placeholder data is used so the demo ledger isn't mistaken for real audit records, given the court-ready/compliance positioning. Reconsider 4s polling on a hidden canvas vs an actual SSE/websocket.

#### [MEDIUM] Landing hero metrics and module mockups are hardcoded marketing data presented as live
`D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\pages\Landing.tsx`

The live / Landing.tsx presents data-flavored UI that is entirely static: the hero live scanner animates a hardcoded risk score to 0.94 via a timer (Landing.tsx:134-145), and stat tiles 284,507 TXN / 1,847 FLAGS / 94.7% RECALL / <200ms (Landing.tsx:228) are literals. StudioMock/RiskMock/NLPMock render fabricated transactions, SHAP waterfalls, and metrics (Landing.tsx:256-339). Normal for a marketing page, but headline numbers (94.7% recall, 0.998 AUC, 284,807 rows) are NOT sourced from /api/risk/metrics and can silently diverge from the actual trained model the Risk page reports. Labels like LIVE NETWORK ANALYSIS (Landing.tsx:659) on a decorative R3F FraudNetwork reinforce a live impression.

**Fix:** Acceptable as marketing but flag drift risk: annotate as illustrative, or (better for a forensic/compliance brand) fetch real headline metrics from /api/risk/metrics so the landing can't advertise stale numbers.

#### [LOW] No 404 / catch-all route; unknown paths render a blank page
`D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\App.tsx`

App.tsx Routes has only / plus the five AppShell children (App.tsx:22-31). There is no path=* fallback, so any unmatched URL (e.g. /studioo, a stale deep link) renders nothing — react-router matches no element and the user sees a blank screen with no nav back. AppShell's sidebar logo links to / (AppShell.tsx:93) which works only if the user is already inside a matched shell route.

**Fix:** Add a <Route path=*> rendering a NotFound (ideally inside the AppShell layout so the sidebar persists), or redirect unknown paths to /overview.

#### [LOW] FloorContentOverlay maps two floors to the same /overview route — detective floor routes to /nlp
`D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\immersive\dom\FloorContentOverlay.tsx`

In the (dead) immersive FLOOR_META (FloorContentOverlay.tsx:19-45): vault->/overview and rooftop->/overview both target the same route, and the DETECTIVE OFFICE floor routes to /nlp (FloorContentOverlay.tsx:30-33) while risk->/risk and studio->/studio are 1:1. The building's five exhibits collapse onto four app routes, and detective-vs-nlp and vault-vs-rooftop labels don't map cleanly to destinations. Not user-visible today (dead code) but a navigation-design inconsistency to resolve before wiring it in.

**Fix:** Audit the floor->route table for intent: give detective/vault distinct destinations or rename floors to match routes; avoid two CTAs silently leading to the same page.

#### [LOW] Studio forecast cache ignores dataset_id/series — stale forecast can show after toggling charts
`D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\pages\Studio.tsx`

In Studio.tsx toggleForecast (lines 128-143), once forecast.data is set it is reused for any chart without refetching: if (forecast.data) { setForecast(...activeId...); return; }. The forecast is keyed only by chartId, not dataset_id/series. The New file handler resets forecast (Studio.tsx:291), mitigating the cross-dataset case, but within a session a forecast computed for one line chart is reused over a different line chart of the same dataset, so a forecast can be displayed over a chart it wasn't computed for. Minor, affects only the forecast overlay.

**Fix:** Key the forecast cache by (dataset_id, chartId) or refetch when activeId changes to a chart whose series differs; at minimum document that the forecast endpoint returns a dataset-level forecast, not per-chart.

#### [NIT] Two independent custom-cursor implementations; theme applied as a module side effect
`D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\store\themeStore.ts` · `D:\RajFiles\Verita\frontend\src\App.tsx`

Cursor handling is split: App.tsx mounts components/CustomCursor on every non-/ route (App.tsx:21), while Landing.tsx ships its own inline Cursor (Landing.tsx:47-75) and the dead immersive layer has a third (UVCursor). Separately, themeStore.ts applies the initial theme as a top-level module side effect (themeStore.ts:17-18, apply(initial)) at import time rather than in React — works, but couples DOM mutation to module evaluation and runs before render. Minor maintainability notes.

**Fix:** Consolidate cursor logic where practical; consider moving initial theme application into an effect/bootstrap to keep module import side-effect-free.

### Frontend build / typecheck / test health

**Grade:** D+ — tests genuinely pass but tsc fails (5 errors) and `npm run test` falsely exits 1; coverage ~5%

> Mixed health. Typecheck (npx tsc --noEmit) FAILS with 5 errors, all in one file (src/pages/Overview.tsx) caused by an untyped fetch helper. Tests pass when vitest runs directly (3 files, 10 tests, all green, exit 0), but the project's `npm run test` script exits 1 with NO test output on Windows — a broken npm-wrapper signal that would make CI red even though tests pass. Test coverage is thin: 3 test files vs 58 source files (~5%). dev-server.log shows a transient (now-fixed) JSX syntax error in DossierNav.tsx and recurring Fast-Refresh warnings, both non-blocking.

#### [HIGH] tsc --noEmit fails: 5 errors in Overview.tsx
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/pages/Overview.tsx`

`npx tsc --noEmit` exits with code 2. All 5 errors are in src/pages/Overview.tsx and share one root cause: the inline fetch helper `const j = (p) => fetch(...).then(r => r.ok ? r.json() : {}).catch(() => ({}))` is inferred as returning `{} | {}`, so destructured values `s`, `a`, `i` have no typed properties. Exact errors:
  src/pages/Overview.tsx(58,32): error TS2339: Property 'analyses' does not exist on type '{} | {}'.
  src/pages/Overview.tsx(58,65): error TS2339: Property 'investigations' does not exist on type '{} | {}'.
  src/pages/Overview.tsx(58,97): error TS2339: Property 'queries' does not exist on type '{} | {}'.
  src/pages/Overview.tsx(59,21): error TS2339: Property 'analyses' does not exist on type '{} | {}'.
  src/pages/Overview.tsx(60,27): error TS2339: Property 'investigations' does not exist on type '{} | {}'.
This means the full `npm run build` (vite build + tsc) is NOT safe to assume green — the task's guard 'only run build if tsc is clean' was not met, so build was not run.

**Fix:** Type the fetch helper's return, e.g. `const j = <T = any>(p: string): Promise<T> => fetch(...).then(r => (r.ok ? r.json() : {})).catch(() => ({} as T))`, or give the Promise.all results explicit types. After fixing, re-run tsc and only then attempt the full build.

#### [HIGH] `npm run test` exits 1 despite all tests passing — CI-breaking
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/package.json`

The package.json script is `"test": "vitest run"`. Running `npm run test` prints only the npm banner (`> verita-frontend@0.1.0 test` / `> vitest run`) and exits with code 1 — no RUN header, no results, no failures. Reproduced twice. Yet invoking the same binary directly (`node ./node_modules/vitest/vitest.mjs run` and `npx vitest run`) succeeds: `Test Files 3 passed (3) / Tests 10 passed (10)`, exit 0. So the tests themselves are healthy; the npm script wrapper is producing a false failure on this Windows/PowerShell environment (npm swallows vitest's stdout and reports a nonzero exit). Any CI step calling `npm run test` or `npm test` will go red.

**Fix:** Investigate the npm wrapper failure (npm version / shell on Windows). As a workaround, change the script to invoke vitest explicitly or via npx (`"test": "npx vitest run"`) or pin to the node entrypoint; verify `npm run test` then exits 0 with visible output.

#### [MEDIUM] Test coverage gap: 3 test files vs 58 source files (~5%)
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/ErrorBoundary.test.tsx` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/components/studio/InsightsPanel.test.tsx` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/lib/chartOptions.test.ts`

Only 3 test files exist: src/components/ErrorBoundary.test.tsx, src/components/studio/InsightsPanel.test.tsx, src/lib/chartOptions.test.ts. Against 58 non-test .ts/.tsx source files in src/. The large src/immersive/* canvas/DOM landing code, all pages (Overview, Risk, NLP, Studio, Settings, Landing), and the API/data layer have no tests. The 10 passing tests give very limited regression protection.

**Fix:** Add tests for the pages and data-fetching logic (especially Overview.tsx, which currently has the tsc errors), and for critical immersive components if they ship to users.

#### [LOW] dev-server.log: transient (now-fixed) JSX syntax error + recurring Fast-Refresh warnings
`D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/dev-server.log` · `D:/RajFiles/Verita/frontend/src/immersive/context/BuildingContext.tsx`

dev-server.log (145 lines) contains one hard error at 10:15:28am: `[vite] Internal server error: src/immersive/dom/DossierNav.tsx: Unterminated JSX contents. (417:17)`. This was a mid-edit HMR error — a later 10:15:35am HMR update recompiled the file, and the current full tsc run does NOT flag DossierNav.tsx, so it is already fixed; it is stale log noise, not a live problem. Also recurring throughout the log: `hmr invalidate /src/immersive/context/BuildingContext.tsx ... Could not Fast Refresh ("useBuildingContext" export is incompatible)`. This is a non-blocking DX warning — the context module exports both a component and a hook, defeating Fast Refresh and forcing full reloads on every edit.

**Fix:** No action needed for the syntax error (already resolved). Optionally, to restore Fast Refresh, move the `useBuildingContext` hook into a separate file from the provider component in src/immersive/context/BuildingContext.tsx.

**Done well:**
- All existing tests pass cleanly when vitest is run directly: 3 files, 10 tests, 0 failures (verified via both `npx vitest run` and the node entrypoint).
- TypeScript errors are tightly localized — all 5 are in a single file (Overview.tsx) with one shared root cause, so they are quick to fix.
- The dev-server JSX error in DossierNav.tsx was already fixed before this audit (not present in current tsc/test runs) — no lingering compile break in tracked code.

## Per-file issue register (340 total)

| Severity | Count |
|---|---|
| high | 19 |
| medium | 77 |
| low | 155 |
| nit | 89 |

_`[immersive/dead]` = issue is in the currently-unrouted immersive/dead-code tree._

### HIGH (19)

- **`frontend/src/components/AppShell.tsx:43-52`** — Timer leak in the chain-seal effect: the cleanup (lines 50-51) only clears the initial setTimeout `t`. The recursive setTimeout handles created inside tick() (line 48) are never stored or cleared, so unmounting AppShell during the ~800–2600ms animation window leaves pending callbacks that fire setSealedNodes on an unmounted component. Compounded by React.StrictMode double-mount in dev (overlapping animation runs).
- **`frontend/src/pages/Risk.tsx:124-128`** — KPI render calls metrics.roc_auc.toFixed(3) etc. with no null-guard. If the backend returns a Metrics object missing any numeric field (or null), this throws a runtime TypeError and the page white-screens. Same risk at 165/168 (toFixed in chart titles) and 90 (cm fields).
- **`frontend/src/sections/Hero.tsx:98-107`** `[immersive/dead]` — Typewriter setInterval leak: the inner interval id is only cleared from inside its own callback when typing completes; the useEffect cleanup return only clears the outer `start` timeout. Unmounting mid-typing keeps the interval firing setN on an unmounted component (React state-update-on-unmounted warning + leak). Should capture and clear the interval in cleanup.
- **`frontend/src/components/EChart.tsx:11,9-19`** — ECharts is initialized exactly once with empty deps, so it is created before/independent of layout in some cases; more importantly there is no handling for the container having zero size at init (common when mounted inside a tab/accordion that is display:none). ECharts logs a 'Can't get DOM width or height' warning and renders at 0x0; the ResizeObserver will fire when it becomes visible, but resize() alone does not re-run an initial layout reliably for all chart types. Consider initializing lazily or re-init on first non-zero size.
- **`frontend/src/immersive/canvas/BuildingScene.tsx:95,110`** `[immersive/dead]` — WindDust useFrame iterates a hardcoded 160 (line 110) instead of deriving from the N=160 used to build the buffer (line 95). The loop count is duplicated as a magic number; if N is ever changed at line 95 without also editing line 110, the loop will under/over-run the typed array (over-run writes past the buffer and silently corrupts/NaNs positions). Derive the count from arr.length/3.
- **`frontend/src/immersive/context/BuildingContext.tsx:66-74,92-101`** `[immersive/dead]` — enterBuilding (69) and teleportTo (96) use setTimeout with NO cleanup. If the BuildingProvider unmounts (route change away from the landing) during the 600ms window, the timer still fires and calls setPhaseState/setCurrentFloor/setTransitioning on an unmounted provider — React will warn and the work is wasted. The timeout IDs are never stored or cleared. There is also no guard against double-invocation (rapid clicks queue multiple overlapping 600ms timers, each flipping isTransitioning false at the end and racing the phase).
- **`frontend/src/immersive/canvas/lobby/LobbyRoom.tsx:16-30`** `[immersive/dead]` — Module-scope shared materials floorMat/wallMat/trimMat/stoneMat/darkMat (lines 16-30) are created at import time and never disposed; they persist for the app lifetime even when the lobby unmounts (LobbySceneContents conditionally renders LobbyRoom). Sharing one material instance across dozens of <primitive object={mat}> meshes means r3f will NOT dispose them on unmount (not created by JSX) — combined with module scope this is permanent retention; a real leak under hot-reload or scene re-creation.
- **`frontend/src/immersive/canvas/lobby/LobbyRoom.tsx:234-241,342,395-401`** `[immersive/dead]` — Very high dynamic-light count: the lobby mounts 14 point lights (8 chandelier pendants + 4 sconces + ceiling fill + pedestal glow) plus the exterior rig's 4 lights and 2 shadow-casting directional maps. Real-time per-pixel lighting with this many lights on meshStandardMaterial is a serious performance trap on mid/low GPUs and mobile, especially with shadows enabled. No light-count budget, baking, or LOD. Consider baking the chandelier/sconce glow into emissive + a couple of representative lights.
- **`frontend/src/immersive/canvas/lobby/LivingLedger.tsx:59-70`** `[immersive/dead]` — pageMat is recreated via useMemo whenever `texture` changes (lines 59-70), but the OLD MeshStandardMaterial is never disposed. If useLiveLedger swaps the texture reference (or the material is recreated), each previous material instance leaks GPU resources. Likewise if the live texture is a CanvasTexture being recreated rather than updated-in-place (texture.needsUpdate=true), the old textures leak. Add disposal on material/texture replacement.
- **`frontend/src/immersive/canvas/exterior/EntranceDoors.tsx:110`** `[immersive/dead]` — Cursor side-effect runs in a per-frame useFrame and is GLOBAL on the canvas: every DoorPanel writes gl.domElement.style.cursor every frame, and both panels default to 'none'. So whenever neither door is hovered the canvas cursor is permanently hidden ('none'), and the two panels race each frame to set the cursor. This both hides the cursor unexpectedly across the whole scene and is an a11y/usability problem (invisible pointer). Setting cursor every frame is also wasteful; should be event-driven (onPointerOver/Out) and restore to 'auto'/'default', not 'none'.
- **`frontend/src/immersive/canvas/exterior/EntranceDoors.tsx:45-62`** `[immersive/dead]` — PaperScatter useFrame never deactivates or recycles. Once scatterActive is true it stays true forever (no reset), so 600 particles are integrated every frame for the life of the scene even after they've flown far off-screen. Perf trap / unbounded animation with no end condition or cleanup.
- **`frontend/src/immersive/canvas/exterior/HashChainSpine.tsx:23-64`** `[immersive/dead]` — Multiple GSAP tweens (emissiveIntensity, color, emissive, scale pop) are started on mount but never killed. No useEffect cleanup returning gsap.killTweensOf(matRef.current)/(meshRef.current). If HashChainSpine unmounts during the ~3s seal sequence (likely on the exterior→teleporting phase change), GSAP continues mutating detached THREE objects. Memory/CPU leak, and on remount the staggered tweens restart with no guarantee the previous ones stopped.
- **`frontend/src/immersive/canvas/materials/uvReveal.ts:27-28`** `[immersive/dead]` — Reveal distance is computed in clip/NDC space (ndc = vClip.xy/vClip.w) and compared against uMouse. NDC is aspect-ratio-unaware, so the reveal 'circle' is actually an ellipse stretched by the viewport aspect ratio (wider on widescreen). Needs aspect correction (multiply x by aspect, or pass resolution uniform) to be a true circle.
- **`frontend/src/immersive/dom/FloorContentOverlay.tsx:21`** `[immersive/dead]` — Route mismatch: FLOOR_META maps detective→'/nlp' but BOTH vault AND rooftop→'/overview' (studio→'/studio', risk→'/risk'). The 'CHAIN VAULT / audit trail' floor (cta 'OPEN AUDIT VAULT') has no dedicated destination — it lands on the same '/overview' as rooftop's 'ENTER PLATFORM', likely a content/route bug or placeholder.
- **`frontend/src/immersive/dom/PaperShredTransition.tsx:187`** `[immersive/dead]` — Effect cleanup cancels rafRef but does NOT kill tlRef (the GSAP timeline). On unmount mid-transition the timeline keeps running; its onUpdate mutates pieces and its onComplete touches canvasRef.current — a use-after-unmount and a leaked timeline. Cleanup should call tlRef.current?.kill().
- **`frontend/src/immersive/dom/PaperShredTransition.tsx:84`** `[immersive/dead]` — Canvas backing store is sized to window.innerWidth/innerHeight only inside this effect (deps [phase]) and there is NO window resize listener. A viewport resize that does not coincide with a phase change leaves the canvas stale, so paper pieces are mispositioned and may fail to cover the screen during the camera snap.
- **`frontend/src/immersive/dom/Preloader.tsx:50`** `[immersive/dead]` — No maximum-time failsafe: if sceneReady NEVER arrives, the driver early-returns at line 50 once progress hits 83, pinning the bar permanently and hanging the boot forever with no timeout/escape. A hard cap fallback would prevent a stuck preloader.
- **`frontend/src/immersive/dom/UVCursor.tsx:38`** `[immersive/dead]` — The RAF spring calls setLaggedPos({...laggedRef.current}) on EVERY frame (~60fps) unconditionally — even when the cursor is idle and laggedRef has already converged on targetRef. This is a continuous per-frame React state update + re-render for the component's whole life, defeating the 'zero overhead' goal stated in the comment (line 12). It should stop the loop (or skip setState) once |target-lagged| is below a threshold.
- **`frontend/src/immersive/hooks/useLiveLedger.ts:223`** `[immersive/dead]` — const ctx = canvas.getContext('2d')! uses a non-null assertion. If the 2d context is unavailable (rare, but possible under canvas exhaustion / headless), ctx is null and the first render() throws inside the RAF loop, breaking the texture silently. No guard.

### MEDIUM (77)

- **`frontend/src/config.ts:1`** — Hardcoded http://localhost:8000 fallback with no .env file present in the repo — a production build with VITE_API_URL unset will silently point the frontend at localhost (mixed-content/CORS failures in prod). No runtime warning.
- **`frontend/src/App.tsx:22-31`** — No catch-all route (path="*"). Unknown URLs render a blank screen (router logs 'No routes matched location') instead of a 404/redirect — poor UX and a dead-end for mistyped/stale links.
- **`frontend/src/App.tsx:5-10`** — All page components are imported eagerly (lines 5-10) with no React.lazy/Suspense code-splitting. The immersive react-three-fiber pages (Landing/Studio) are bundled into the initial load, inflating first-paint cost for users who only need a 2D page.
- **`frontend/src/components/AppShell.tsx:362-388`** — Theme-toggle button has no :focus / focus-visible styling — hover feedback is applied imperatively via onMouseEnter/onMouseLeave (lines 378-385) only, so keyboard users get no visible focus indicator. Violates the project's stated WCAG AAA bar (CLAUDE.md / PRODUCT.md).
- **`frontend/src/components/AppShell.tsx:362-388, 252-294`** — Nav links and the theme toggle rely on color/border + an 'OPEN' text tag for active state but the toggle button's accessible name is only the title attribute 'Toggle theme'; it has no aria-pressed/aria-label reflecting current theme, and the decorative hash/chain elements lack aria-hidden, adding noise for screen readers.
- **`frontend/src/components/AppShell.tsx:23-32`** — useHashTick setInterval (lines 26-29) runs unconditionally every 2200ms for the life of the shell, ignoring prefers-reduced-motion. The index.css reduced-motion query (lines 717-723) only kills CSS animations/transitions; the JS-driven hash flicker continues, so motion-sensitive users still see the value change. Also keeps timers alive in background tabs.
- **`frontend/src/pages/Overview.tsx:54-63`** — useEffect has no AbortController and no isMounted guard; if the component unmounts before Promise.all resolves, the four setState calls fire on an unmounted component (React 18 dev warning / potential update-after-unmount). No cleanup returned.
- **`frontend/src/pages/Risk.tsx:62-66`** — On threshold changes after first load, a fetch failure sets error but the render path never shows it (all three error/loading guards require !metrics). User silently sees stale metrics with no indication the live recompute failed.
- **`frontend/src/pages/Risk.tsx:59-60`** — fetch().then(r=>r.json()) does not check r.ok; a 4xx/5xx returning a JSON error body resolves 'successfully' and is fed into setMetrics/setAlerts as if valid, bypassing the catch. Only network-level failures reach the error handler.
- **`frontend/src/pages/NLP.tsx:280`** — highlight() is called with the live `text` state, not a snapshot of the analyzed text. Editing the textarea after a successful analyze (without re-running) leaves stale result.entities start/end offsets pointing into mismatched text, producing misaligned/garbled highlights or dropped spans. The analyzed text should be captured alongside result.
- **`frontend/src/pages/NLP.tsx:84-85`** — catch (e:any) discards the real error (including the precise `Server ${status}` thrown at 82) and always shows 'Backend unavailable'. A 500/422 from a reachable backend is misreported as the backend being down. e:any also defeats type-checking.
- **`frontend/src/pages/Settings.tsx:32-34`** — Health useEffect has no cleanup/AbortController; if Settings unmounts before /api/health resolves, setHealth/setErr fire post-unmount. Same class of issue as Overview.
- **`frontend/src/pages/Studio.tsx:128-143, 339-378`** — Forecast data is cached globally (single forecast.data) but keyed to one activeId. Toggling forecast on a different line chart reuses the FIRST chart's forecast series rather than fetching that chart's own forecast — every line chart shows the same forecast overlay. If multiple line charts exist this is incorrect data.
- **`frontend/src/pages/Studio.tsx:187`** — setTimeout(1600ms) that commits result/phase has no cleanup. If the component unmounts (route change) during the reveal window, the timer fires setState on an unmounted component — React warning / potential leak. No ref/clearTimeout guard.
- **`frontend/src/pages/Studio.tsx:143`** — toggleForecast useCallback depends on the whole `forecast` object, so it is recreated on every forecast state change (loading, data, activeId), defeating memoization and re-creating the handler passed to each panel button repeatedly.
- **`frontend/src/components/studio/Investigator.tsx:55-84`** — The idle/running/error early-return only covers those three phases. A transient error during a RE-RUN replaces the whole done view (verdict/trace/memo) with the empty intro card, losing the visible prior result even though it's still in state.
- **`frontend/src/components/studio/Investigator.tsx:47-48`** — Artificial await setTimeout(700) on every run adds purely cosmetic latency to a compliance tool, and has no cleanup if unmounted mid-pause (setState-after-unmount risk).
- **`frontend/src/components/studio/SqlPlayground.tsx:14-17, 45-62`** — Doc comment claims the NL translation is 'shown for review, never auto-run blindly', but translate() calls `await run(body.sql)` immediately after setSql — it DOES auto-run the model-generated SQL. Behavior contradicts the stated safety promise.
- **`frontend/src/components/studio/GeoMap.tsx:29-37`** — fetch() has no AbortController / no cleanup. If GeoMap unmounts before the CDN GeoJSON resolves, the .then calls setState on an unmounted component (React warns) and registers the map post-unmount. Add an abort controller or `cancelled` guard.
- **`frontend/src/components/studio/GeoMap.tsx:17,32`** — World GeoJSON is loaded from a hardcoded third-party CDN (cdn.jsdelivr.net/npm/echarts@4.9.0/map/json/world.json). External network dependency: blocked CDN, CSP, or offline => silent fallback to a bar list. Version 4.9.0 is also decoupled from the project's installed echarts version. Consider bundling the geojson locally.
- **`frontend/src/components/studio/TimeMachine.tsx:24-31`** — fetch effect has no AbortController/cleanup and no loading or error UI. Switching datasetId rapidly or unmounting mid-flight can setFrames on an unmounted component (React state-on-unmount warning) and create a race where a stale response overwrites a newer one. On failure frames stays [] and the component silently renders null (catch at line 31) — a genuine backend/network error is indistinguishable from 'dataset has <2 periods', with no logging.
- **`frontend/src/components/studio/WhatChanged.tsx:22-30`** — fetch has no AbortController/cleanup; rapid datasetId changes or unmount mid-flight can cause setState-on-unmounted and a stale-response race (older response overwriting newer). Same pattern as TimeMachine.
- **`frontend/src/components/studio/WhatChanged.tsx:32`** — `if (error) return null` swallows ALL fetch failures — a real network/500 error is treated identically to the intended 'no usable time axis' case, with no logging and no user feedback. A backend outage makes the panel silently vanish.
- **`frontend/src/pages/Landing.tsx:56-62`** — Cursor RAF loop (requestAnimationFrame(tick)) is never cancelled — cleanup only removes the mousemove listener, not the RAF. On unmount the loop keeps running; ref-guards are the only thing preventing errors. Memory/CPU leak across route changes.
- **`frontend/src/pages/Landing.tsx:353-358, 410-415, 466-471`** — SectionStudio/Risk/NLP fire gsap.from() entrance tweens NOT wrapped in gsap.context; only the IntersectionObserver is disconnected on cleanup. Unmounting mid-tween orphans the tween (inline transforms may be left applied). Inconsistent with the ctx-wrapped tweens elsewhere.
- **`frontend/src/sections/LedgerSections.tsx:289`** `[immersive/dead]` — Exhibit Entry numbering `000${i+3}` is fragile — only correct because EXHIBITS has exactly 5 items (yields 0003-0007). A 7th+ exhibit produces malformed numbers like '00010'. Should zero-pad with padStart, not string interpolation.
- **`frontend/src/components/CustomCursor.tsx:19-33`** — matchMedia is evaluated only once on mount; there is no MediaQueryList 'change' listener. If the user toggles prefers-reduced-motion at the OS level, or switches input modality (e.g. attaches a mouse to a tablet / Windows tablet-mode flip), `enabled` never updates without a remount. a11y/responsiveness gap.
