- _nit_ (line 47,75): Both layers hardcode zIndex 9999 with no documented stacking contract; if any modal/overlay uses a comparable z-index the decorative cursor could paint over it (pointerEvents:none mitigates interaction issues but not visual layering).

**Dead code / TODOs:** None. No TODOs, no mock data, no commented-out code.

### `frontend/src/components/EChart.tsx`  _(26 lines)_

**Purpose:** Minimal imperative wrapper around the Apache ECharts library: initializes a chart instance once into a div ref, pushes `option` updates, observes container resize, and disposes on unmount. Lets the rest of the app render charts declaratively by passing an ECharts option object.
**Exports:** `default EChart (React.FC<{ option: echarts.EChartsOption; height?: number | string }>)`
**Props/inputs:** `option: echarts.EChartsOption` (required) — the full ECharts config. `height?: number | string` (default 240) — container height; width is always 100%.
**State/refs:** No React state. Two refs: `ref` (useRef<HTMLDivElement>) for the mount node; `chart` (useRef<echarts.ECharts | null>, init null) holding the imperative chart instance across renders.
**Hooks:** `useRef (x2)`, `useEffect (x2)`
**Dependencies:** `react (useEffect, useRef)`, `echarts (namespace import * as echarts)`
**Data flow:** Pure props-in. The `option` object is provided by the parent; no fetching or mock data here. ResizeObserver feeds container size changes back to chart.resize().
**Styling:** Single inline style on the div: width 100%, height from prop (default 240). No classes; ECharts manages canvas rendering internally (renderer: 'canvas').

**Details:** Init effect (lines 9-19, empty deps — runs once): guards on ref.current; calls echarts.init(el, undefined /* theme */, { renderer: 'canvas' }); stores instance in chart.current; creates a ResizeObserver that calls chart.current?.resize() and observes the container. Cleanup disconnects the observer, disposes the chart, and nulls the ref. Option effect (lines 21-23, dep [option]): calls chart.current?.setOption(option, true) — the SECOND arg `true` is ECharts' `notMerge` flag, meaning each update REPLACES the option rather than merging. This avoids stale-series leakage but discards any internal incremental-update optimizations and can cause full re-render/flicker and loss of internal UI state (e.g. legend toggles, dataZoom range) on every option change.

**Issues:**
- _high_ (line 11,9-19): ECharts is initialized exactly once with empty deps, so it is created before/independent of layout in some cases; more importantly there is no handling for the container having zero size at init (common when mounted inside a tab/accordion that is display:none). ECharts logs a 'Can't get DOM width or height' warning and renders at 0x0; the ResizeObserver will fire when it becomes visible, but resize() alone does not re-run an initial layout reliably for all chart types. Consider initializing lazily or re-init on first non-zero size.
- _medium_ (line 22): setOption(option, true) uses notMerge=true on every option change. Because `option` is a prop, any parent re-render that creates a NEW option object literal (the common case) triggers a full non-merge replacement — destroying internal interaction state (legend selection, dataZoom window, tooltip) and incurring a full redraw. If callers pass inline object literals this fires far more than intended.
- _medium_ (line 21-23): Referential-identity perf trap: the effect depends on `option` by reference. Callers must memoize the option object or every render re-applies it. There is no useMemo guidance and no shallow comparison; an unmemoized inline `option={{...}}` causes setOption on every render.
- _low_ (line 11): Hardcoded renderer 'canvas' with no SVG option and no `theme` (undefined). Fine as a default, but not configurable via props — callers cannot opt into SVG rendering or a theme without editing this wrapper.
- _low_ (line 9-19): No global window 'resize' fallback. The ResizeObserver covers container-size changes (the right approach), but device-pixel-ratio changes (e.g. dragging the window between monitors with different DPR) are not observed and won't trigger a crisp re-render.
- _nit_ (line 5): No accessibility affordance: the chart canvas has no role, aria-label, or text fallback. ECharts can emit an aria description via option.aria, but the wrapper neither sets nor exposes it — a11y gap against the project's stated WCAG AAA bar.

**Dead code / TODOs:** None. No TODOs or dead code.

### `frontend/src/components/ErrorBoundary.tsx`  _(38 lines)_

**Purpose:** React error boundary (the app's only class component) that catches render-phase errors in its subtree so a single broken view degrades gracefully into an inline error card instead of blanking the entire app. Provides a 'Try again' reset.
**Exports:** `default ErrorBoundary (React.Component<Props, State> class)`
**Props/inputs:** `children: ReactNode` (required, the wrapped subtree). `label?: string` (optional, names the view in the logged message and the error card copy).
**State/refs:** Class state `{ error: Error | null }`, initialized to { error: null } at line 9. Set via getDerivedStateFromError on a thrown error; reset to null by the Try again button.
**Hooks:** `None (class component). Lifecycle: getDerivedStateFromError (static), componentDidCatch.`
**Dependencies:** `react (Component, type ReactNode)`, `lucide-react (AlertTriangle icon)`
**Data flow:** No API/mock data. Error/info flow in from React's error-handling lifecycle; `label` and `children` flow in from the parent that wraps a view.
**Styling:** Uses the legacy '.glass' CSS class for the card plus inline styles. Theme tokens via CSS vars: --danger (icon), --font-display (heading), --text-muted (message), --signature (button background). Hardcoded: margin 24, padding 32, button '10px 20px', borderRadius 10, color #fff, fontWeight 600.

**Details:** Standard boundary pattern. getDerivedStateFromError (lines 11-13) maps a thrown error to { error } so the fallback renders on the next commit. componentDidCatch (lines 15-18) logs `[Verita] error in {label||'view'}:` with the error and the (typed `unknown`) info object — no external error-reporting/telemetry, console only. render (lines 20-37): if no error, returns children untouched; otherwise renders the fallback card with an AlertTriangle (size 32, color var(--danger)), an h2 'Something went wrong in this view', a paragraph showing `{label}: {error.message}` (label prefix only if provided), and a 'Try again' button whose onClick calls setState({ error: null }) to clear the error and re-attempt rendering the children.

**Issues:**
- _medium_ (line 29-34): 'Try again' simply resets error to null and re-renders the SAME children. If the underlying error is deterministic (bad prop, null data), it will immediately re-throw and reproduce the fallback — an infinite manual-retry loop with no remount/key-reset or backoff. A `resetKeys`/onReset-with-remount pattern would actually recover; as-is the button is often a no-op for persistent errors.
- _medium_ (line 15-18): componentDidCatch only console.errors — there is no integration with any error-reporting/telemetry service. Production render crashes are invisible beyond the user's own devtools console. (Acceptable for a demo, but a real gap for a compliance product.)
- _low_ (line 27): The raw error.message is rendered directly into the UI. For an internal tool this is fine, but surfacing raw exception text to end users can leak implementation details / stack-adjacent info and is not localized.
- _low_ (line 23-35): Accessibility: the error card has no role="alert" / aria-live region, so screen readers are not notified when a view crashes and the fallback appears. The decorative AlertTriangle icon also lacks aria-hidden. Falls short of the project's WCAG AAA bar.
- _low_ (line 29-34): The 'Try again' button uses inline styles only and lacks any :focus-visible / hover styling defined here, and there is no keyboard-focus management when the boundary trips (focus is not moved to the alert), reducing keyboard/AT usability.
- _nit_ (line 15): `info` is typed as `unknown` rather than React's ErrorInfo type; harmless since it is only forwarded to console, but loses type information (e.g. info.componentStack).

**Dead code / TODOs:** None functionally dead. componentDidCatch logs to console.error with an eslint-disable-next-line no-console pragma (line 16) — intentional, not dead code. No TODOs.

### `frontend/src/components/SignalField.tsx`  _(85 lines)_

**Purpose:** Decorative react-three-fiber background: a 'financial signal field' of ~2600 glowing points (an abstract transaction graph) arranged in a flattened ellipsoid that rotates continuously, breathes (scale oscillation), and parallaxes toward the cursor. Uses additive blending to fake bloom without a postprocessing pass.
**Exports:** `default SignalField (React.FC, no props) — wraps the inner Points mesh in a Canvas. (Points is module-private, not exported.)`
**Props/inputs:** SignalField takes no props. Points takes no props.
**State/refs:** No React state. Refs: `ref` (useRef<THREE.Points>) in Points for the points object manipulated each frame. `pointer` from useThree() provides normalized cursor coords for parallax. Geometry buffers cached in useMemo.
**Hooks:** `useMemo (geometry/color generation)`, `useRef`, `useFrame (@react-three/fiber per-frame loop)`, `useThree (@react-three/fiber)`
**Dependencies:** `react (useMemo, useRef)`, `@react-three/fiber (Canvas, useFrame, useThree)`, `three (* as THREE)`
**Data flow:** No API/props. All data procedurally generated once in useMemo: COUNT=2600 points; positions distributed with r = pow(random,0.6)*9 (radius bias toward the rim), spherical-to-cartesian with y flattened by 0.55 for an ellipsoid; per-point color lerped along the cyan/blue/violet gradient by normalized radius t=r/9. Cursor data comes from useThree().pointer.
**Styling:** Inline style on Canvas: position absolute, inset 0, zIndex 1 (sits behind content). Visual look driven by Three.js material props (pointsMaterial: size 0.045, vertexColors, transparent, opacity 0.9, sizeAttenuation, depthWrite false, AdditiveBlending). Color gradient cyan #22d3ee -> blue #4d7cff -> violet #a855f7 by radius.

**Details:** Points geometry (useMemo, lines 16-39, empty deps so generated once): allocates two Float32Array(COUNT*3). For each point: r uses Math.pow(Math.random(),0.6)*9 to bias density outward; theta uniform 0..2pi; phi = acos(2*random-1) for uniform sphere sampling; cartesian x/y/z with y multiplied by 0.55 to flatten into an ellipsoid; colors computed by splitting t=r/9 at 0.5 — inner half lerps cInner->cMid (t*2), outer half lerps cMid->cOuter ((t-0.5)*2). useFrame (lines 41-53) runs every frame: guards on ref.current; rotation.y += delta*0.06 and rotation.z += delta*0.015 for continuous spin (delta-scaled = frame-rate independent); breathing scale s = 1 + sin(elapsedTime*0.4)*0.03 applied via scale.setScalar; parallax: rotation.x eased toward pointer.y*0.25 by factor 0.04 (proper lerp), then rotation.y += pointer.x*0.25*0.01 (NOTE: this ADDS to rotation.y rather than easing, see issues). Canvas (lines 74-84): camera position [0,0,14] fov 60; dpr clamped [1,2]; gl antialias true, alpha true (transparent background to show content behind). Magic numbers: COUNT 2600, r exponent 0.6, radius 9, y-flatten 0.55, gradient split 0.5, rotation speeds 0.06/0.015, breathe 0.4/0.03, parallax 0.25/0.04/0.01, point size 0.045, opacity 0.9, camera z 14, fov 60.

**Issues:**
- _medium_ (line 51-52): Inconsistent parallax math: rotation.x is eased toward a target (`+= (pointer.y*0.25 - g.rotation.x) * 0.04`, a stable lerp), but rotation.y is INCREMENTED by `pointer.x*0.25*0.01` every frame on top of the continuous spin from line 45. Because it adds rather than easing toward a target, holding the cursor to one side continuously accelerates/biases the Y spin instead of settling at a parallax offset — likely not the intended 'parallax toward cursor' behavior (asymmetric with the X axis).
- _medium_ (line 74-85): No reduced-motion handling: the field rotates, breathes, and parallaxes unconditionally. Unlike CustomCursor (which respects prefers-reduced-motion), this continuous animation runs for all users, conflicting with the project's WCAG AAA accessibility bar (2.3.3 Animation from Interactions / motion sensitivity).
- _medium_ (line 74-85): No visibility/idle gating: the Canvas + useFrame render loop runs continuously even when the component is offscreen, scrolled away, or the tab is backgrounded (r3f defaults to always-on frameloop). Constant GPU/CPU cost for a purely decorative layer; an on-demand frameloop or IntersectionObserver pause would save power, especially on laptops/mobile.
- _low_ (line 76-83): No aria/role on the Canvas and no aria-hidden; the decorative WebGL canvas is exposed to the accessibility tree with no label. Should be aria-hidden='true' since it is purely ornamental.
- _low_ (line 76): No WebGL-context-loss handling and no fallback if WebGL is unavailable/blocked. If context creation fails the background silently disappears (and could throw inside r3f); a static gradient fallback would be more robust. Pairs well with wrapping this in the ErrorBoundary primitive.
- _low_ (line 10): COUNT=2600 points with sizeAttenuation + additive blending + dpr up to 2 is a fixed cost regardless of device; there is no quality/COUNT scaling for low-end or mobile GPUs, so the same particle budget runs everywhere.
- _nit_ (line 13): `useRef<THREE.Points>(null!)` uses the non-null assertion init pattern; conventional for r3f refs but technically lies about the initial null value (mitigated by the `if (!g) return` guard in useFrame).

**Dead code / TODOs:** None. No TODOs or mock placeholders (the procedural data IS the intended content, not a placeholder).

---

## Immersive 3D Landing — Scene Root, Building Context State Machine, and Lobby (react-three-fiber)

> This subsystem is the entry point and 3D scaffolding for Verita's "immersive building" landing experience. ImmersiveLanding wraps everything in a BuildingProvider and orchestrates a phased intro (preloader -> stamp -> exterior -> enter -> lobby -> floors -> rooftop) using DOM overlays layered over a single full-screen react-three-fiber Canvas. BuildingContext is a finite state machine over a BuildingPhase union plus a currentFloor; it exposes navigation actions (enterBuilding, goToFloor, exitFloor, teleportTo) and a render-free mouse-NDC ref consumed by useFrame loops. BuildingScene builds the persistent r3f scene (camera, golden-hour lighting, fog/background controller, ground/city, exterior facade pieces, particle dust, arrival pulse light, conditionally-mounted LobbyRoom, and a lazy FloorScene). LobbyRoom and LivingLedger are pure procedural geometry: a tiled marble floor, paneled walls, coffered ceiling, chandelier, pillars, sconces, reception desk, and a floating "Living Ledger" book whose left page renders a live canvas texture from the useLiveLedger hook. No real API/data lives in these five files except the live ledger texture pulled via useLiveLedger; everything else is hand-placed geometry and magic-number layout.

### `frontend/src/immersive/ImmersiveLanding.tsx`  _(77 lines)_

**Purpose:** Top-level component for the immersive landing route. Mounts the BuildingProvider, the persistent 3D Canvas (BuildingScene), and all DOM overlays (preloader, stamp intro, UV cursor, nav, HUD, transition, floor content, exterior hint). Drives the preloader -> stamp -> exterior phase handoff.
**Exports:** `ImmersiveLanding (default)`
**Props/inputs:** ImmersiveLanding takes no props. Internal ImmersiveContent takes no props.
**State/refs:** ImmersiveContent local state: preloaderDone (boolean, default false), stampDone (boolean, default false). Reads sceneReady and setPhase from useBuildingContext. No refs.
**Hooks:** `useState (preloaderDone, stampDone)`, `useCallback (handlePreloaderComplete, handleStampComplete)`, `useBuildingContext (custom context hook)`
**Dependencies:** `./context/BuildingContext (BuildingProvider, useBuildingContext)`, `./canvas/BuildingScene`, `./dom/Preloader`, `./dom/StampIntro`, `./dom/UVCursor`, `./dom/DossierNav`, `./dom/ElevatorHUD`, `./dom/PaperShredTransition`, `./dom/FloorContentOverlay`, `./dom/ExteriorHint`
**Data flow:** No API or mock data. Pure orchestration: receives sceneReady from context (set by BuildingScene's ReadySignal), pushes phase transitions back into context via setPhase. Children consume context independently. Callbacks flow down to Preloader/StampIntro and back up as onComplete events.
**Styling:** Inline style object only. Root div: position relative, 100vw x 100vh, overflow hidden, cursor:none (because UVCursor is a custom DOM cursor), background #f3eee2 (paper cream, matches Canvas clear color). Canvas wrapper div: position absolute, inset 0. No CSS classes, no design tokens — hardcoded hex matches DESIGN.md paper palette but is not tokenized.

**Details:** Two-component file: ImmersiveContent (inner, consumes context) and default ImmersiveLanding (outer, provides context). Render order / z-stacking is implicit by DOM order inside the relative container: (1) BuildingScene wrapper at inset 0, (2) Preloader, (3) StampIntro, (4) ExteriorHint, (5) DossierNav, (6) ElevatorHUD, (7) PaperShredTransition, (8) FloorContentOverlay, (9) UVCursor last so it paints on top. Phase gating: Preloader renders only while !preloaderDone (lines 45-47) and is handed sceneReady so it can block until both scene-ready AND its own progress complete. handlePreloaderComplete (18-21) sets preloaderDone and pushes phase to 'stamp_intro'. StampIntro renders only when preloaderDone && !stampDone (50-52); handleStampComplete (23-26) sets stampDone and pushes phase to 'exterior_approach'. After that the intro components are permanently unmounted (no state ever flips them back). ExteriorHint, DossierNav, ElevatorHUD, PaperShredTransition, FloorContentOverlay, UVCursor are always mounted and self-gate on phase internally. The comment at line 54 ('appears 2s after reaching exterior_idle') documents behavior that lives inside ExteriorHint, not here. BuildingScene is mounted immediately and unconditionally (40-42) so the WebGL context is alive during the preloader. Note the initial context phase is 'preloading' but this component never calls setPhase('preloading') — it relies on the provider default; the first explicit transition is to 'stamp_intro'.

**Issues:**
- _medium_ (line 35): Root container sets cursor:'none' (line 35) unconditionally for the whole viewport. If UVCursor fails to mount, errors, or is disabled for reduced-motion/a11y, the user has no visible pointer at all. This is also an accessibility concern against the project's stated WCAG AAA bar — there is no fallback cursor and no prefers-reduced-motion accommodation here.
- _low_ (line 45-52): Intro components are gated purely on local boolean state (preloaderDone/stampDone) that only ever moves forward. If phase were ever reset to 'preloading'/'stamp_intro' elsewhere, the overlays would not re-appear, so the context phase and these local flags can desynchronize. The single source of truth is split between local state and context.phase.
- _low_ (line 40-42): No error boundary around BuildingScene. A throw inside the r3f tree (e.g. a failed lazy FloorScene chunk) would unmount the whole landing with no fallback UI; the cursor:none page would be left blank.
- _nit_ (line 36): Hardcoded paper-cream hex '#f3eee2' is duplicated here and in BuildingScene (clear color + background). Not pulled from a shared token, so the two can drift.

**Dead code / TODOs:** No TODOs or dead code. cursor:'none' on the root is a hard dependency on UVCursor always rendering a visible cursor — see issues.

### `frontend/src/immersive/canvas/BuildingScene.tsx`  _(234 lines)_

**Purpose:** The react-three-fiber Canvas root. Defines camera, renderer/GL settings, golden-hour lighting rig, fog+background, static environment (ground, sidewalk, city silhouettes, building facade, hash-chain spine, entrance doors), animated wind dust, a floor-arrival flash light, the scene-ready signal, the camera-journey controller, the conditionally-mounted lobby, and the lazy floor scene.
**Exports:** `BuildingScene (default)`
**Props/inputs:** BuildingScene takes no props. Internal sub-components: ExteriorLighting (none), Ground (none), CityBackground (none), WindDust (none), FloorArrivalPulse (none), CameraController (none), ReadySignal ({ onReady: () => void }), FogAndBgController (none), LobbySceneContents (none).
**State/refs:** No React state. Refs: WindDust uses useMemo for geometry (no ref). FloorArrivalPulse uses lightRef (THREE.PointLight) and prevRef (last phase). ReadySignal uses firedRef (boolean latch). All animation is via useFrame mutation, not state.
**Hooks:** `useCallback (handleReady)`, `useEffect (FloorArrivalPulse phase-arrival detector; FogAndBgController fog/bg sync)`, `useMemo (WindDust geometry)`, `useRef (lightRef, prevRef, firedRef)`, `useFrame (WindDust drift, FloorArrivalPulse decay, ReadySignal one-shot)`, `useThree (FogAndBgController gets scene)`, `useBuildingContext (phase + setSceneReady)`, `useCameraJourney (custom hook, via CameraController)`
**Dependencies:** `@react-three/fiber (Canvas, useFrame, useThree)`, `three (THREE namespace)`, `../context/BuildingContext`, `./exterior/BuildingFacade`, `./exterior/HashChainSpine`, `./exterior/EntranceDoors`, `./lobby/LobbyRoom`, `./floors/FloorScene`, `../hooks/useCameraJourney`
**Data flow:** No API/mock data. Reads phase from context to drive conditional rendering (WindDust, LobbySceneContents) and effects (FogAndBgController, FloorArrivalPulse). Writes sceneReady=true exactly once via setSceneReady when the first frame renders (ReadySignal). Camera motion is delegated to useCameraJourney. Geometry/colors are all hardcoded literals.
**Styling:** r3f/Three materials only (meshStandardMaterial, pointsMaterial). Canvas DOM style is inline (position absolute, inset 0, 100%/100%). Color palette is hardcoded warm/golden hex strings throughout. GL: ACESFilmic tone mapping, exposure 1.1, antialias true, alpha false, high-performance power preference, shadows enabled, dpr clamp [1,2].

**Details:** Canvas config (200-213): camera position [0,8,62] (far back on +Z, looking toward the building at z≈-22), fov 55, near 0.1, far 400. dpr clamped [1,2] to cap retina cost. gl.alpha=false (opaque). Tone mapping ACESFilmic at exposure 1.1. shadows enabled (PCFSoft default). A <color attach=background> and <fog attach=fog> are declared as children (214-216) AND also mutated imperatively by FogAndBgController — the declarative fog args ['#f0ead8',55,160] are the exterior defaults; the effect overrides to night values on rooftop. ExteriorLighting (13-40): a key directional 'sun' at [10,8,18] intensity 2.6 warm #ffd080 with a 2048² shadow map, ortho shadow frustum left/right/top/bottom ±30, near 0.5 / far 150, shadow-bias -0.0005; plus ambient 1.4 #fff5e0, hemisphere [#ffe8b0 sky, #c8b090 ground] 0.5, and a fill directional from the left [-8,4,10] 0.55. Total ambient+directional is bright — relies on ACES to avoid blowout. Ground (43-63): main 400x400 plane rotated flat at y=-9.5 (the canonical floor level reused as LOBBY_Y); a 20x18 sidewalk slab at z=-10 y=-9.48 (0.02 above ground to avoid z-fight); a thin curb box at z=-1.5 y=-9.35. CityBackground (66-87): 8 silhouette boxes from a tuple array [x,h,z,w,d]; positioned at y = h/2 - 9.5 so each sits on the ground plane; all far back (z -80..-95), flat matte #d0c8b8 roughness 1. WindDust (90-125): only active when phase is exterior_approach/exterior_idle. Builds a 160-point BufferGeometry once (useMemo) with random x in ±27.5, y in -9..11, z in -5..-40. useFrame drifts each point -X by delta*(0.4 + (i%7)*0.18) and +Y by delta*(0.04 + (i%5)*0.025), wrapping X<-27 to 27 and Y>12 to -9, then flags needsUpdate. Guards both the useFrame (early return when not exterior) and the JSX (returns null when not exterior). pointsMaterial size 0.08 sizeAttenuation, opacity 0.42, fog-affected. FloorArrivalPulse (128-148): a pointLight at [0,5,-25] starting intensity 0; useEffect compares prevRef phase to detect transition teleporting -> (any floor_* | lobby | rooftop) and if so slams intensity to 4.5; useFrame decays intensity toward 0 at rate delta*7 (~0.64s to fade from 4.5). prevRef updates every phase change. CameraController (151-154): calls useCameraJourney() and renders null. ReadySignal (157-165): firedRef latch; on the very first useFrame it calls onReady() once and never again — this is how sceneReady flips true (first rendered frame ≈ scene mounted). FogAndBgController (168-183): useEffect keyed on [phase,scene]; if scene.fog is a THREE.Fog, sets color/near/far to night (#040810, 90, 240) on rooftop else day (#f0ead8, 55, 160); if scene.background is a THREE.Color sets #020510 (roof) else #f3eee2. LobbySceneContents (186-191): renders <LobbyRoom/> only when phase is entering | lobby | teleporting | elevator_traveling, otherwise null (so lobby geometry is unmounted on exterior and on dedicated floors). FloorScene (231) is rendered always and 'uses React.lazy internally and provides its own Suspense' per the comment. handleReady (196-198) memoized wrapper around setSceneReady(true).

**Issues:**
- _high_ (line 95,110): WindDust useFrame iterates a hardcoded 160 (line 110) instead of deriving from the N=160 used to build the buffer (line 95). The loop count is duplicated as a magic number; if N is ever changed at line 95 without also editing line 110, the loop will under/over-run the typed array (over-run writes past the buffer and silently corrupts/NaNs positions). Derive the count from arr.length/3.
- _medium_ (line 94-105,121): WindDust BufferGeometry is created in useMemo([]) but never disposed. When phase leaves exterior the <points> unmounts, but r3f only auto-disposes geometry it created from JSX args, not a geometry passed via the `geometry` prop. This geometry (and its attribute buffers) leaks on unmount. Add a disposal effect.
- _medium_ (line 157-165): ReadySignal fires onReady on the FIRST rendered frame, which is not the same as 'all assets loaded'. Lazy FloorScene, textures (useLiveLedger), and the facade may still be streaming when sceneReady flips true, so the Preloader can dismiss before the scene is visually complete. 'Scene ready' here means 'first paint', not 'loaded'.
- _low_ (line 133-139): FloorArrivalPulse arrival detection depends on prevRef being exactly 'teleporting' immediately before the floor phase. enterBuilding() in the context also goes teleporting -> lobby, so entering the lobby triggers the flash too (likely intended). But goToFloor()/exitFloor() transition WITHOUT going through 'teleporting', so direct floor switches that bypass teleportTo never flash — inconsistent arrival feedback depending on which navigation path was used.
- _low_ (line 24-30): Sun directional light at [10,8,18] has its shadow ortho frustum fixed at ±30 with far=150, but the building/lobby interior extends to z≈-47 and the ground is 400 wide; objects outside the ±30 frustum (city silhouettes, distant ground) cast no shadows and may show abrupt shadow-map cutoff. Magic frustum not matched to scene extent.
- _low_ (line 17-31): Two 2048x2048 shadow maps plus ~13 point lights downstream (chandelier 8, sconces 4, pedestal, arrival, lobby fill) — none of the point lights cast shadows (default false) so cost is bounded, but the standalone-material + per-pendant pointLight pattern in the lobby combined with this rig is heavy for low-end GPUs. dpr is capped at 2 which helps but there is no quality tier / reduced setting.
- _nit_ (line 98-114): WindDust wrap logic is asymmetric: X spawns in ±27.5 but wraps at <-27 to +27, and Y spawns -9..11 but wraps >12 to -9. The 0.5-unit and 1-unit mismatches between spawn range and wrap thresholds are harmless but make the bounds look unintentional.
- _nit_ (line 79-84): CityBackground meshes use index `i` as React key (line 80); fine because the array is static, but they are flat boxes that never animate and could be merged/instanced for fewer draw calls.

**Dead code / TODOs:** No TODOs. The declarative <fog>/<color> children are partially redundant with FogAndBgController's imperative mutation (both set the exterior defaults) but not dead — the declarative ones establish the initial Fog/Color instances the effect then mutates.

### `frontend/src/immersive/context/BuildingContext.tsx`  _(128 lines)_

**Purpose:** React context providing the building's phase state machine, current floor, transition flags, scene-ready flag, a non-reactive mouse-NDC ref, and the navigation action set (enterBuilding, goToFloor, exitFloor, teleportTo). Single source of truth for where the user is in the immersive journey.
**Exports:** `BuildingPhase (type)`, `FloorId (type)`, `useBuildingContext (hook)`, `BuildingProvider (component)`
**Props/inputs:** BuildingProvider: { children: React.ReactNode }. useBuildingContext: no args, returns BuildingContextValue or throws.
**State/refs:** useState: phase (BuildingPhase, default 'preloading'), currentFloor (FloorId|null, default null), isTransitioning (boolean, default false), sceneReady (boolean, default false). useRef: mouseNDCRef ({x,y}, default {0,0}) — intentionally non-reactive.
**Hooks:** `createContext/useContext`, `useState x4`, `useRef (mouseNDCRef)`, `useCallback (setPhase, setMouseNDC, enterBuilding, floorPhase, goToFloor, exitFloor, teleportTo)`, `useMemo (context value)`
**Dependencies:** `react (createContext, useContext, useState, useRef, useCallback, useMemo)`
**Data flow:** No external data. Pure in-memory state machine. mouseNDC is written by a DOM mousemove handler elsewhere (e.g. UVCursor / camera hook) and read inside useFrame loops without re-rendering. Phase transitions are triggered by overlay components (ImmersiveLanding intro, DossierNav, ElevatorHUD) and consumed by the entire r3f tree.
**Styling:** None — logic only.

**Details:** BuildingPhase union (3-16) has 12 members: preloading, stamp_intro, exterior_approach, exterior_idle, entering, lobby, elevator_traveling, floor_studio, floor_risk, floor_detective, floor_vault, rooftop, teleporting. FloorId union (18): lobby, studio, risk, detective, vault, rooftop. useBuildingContext (39-43) throws if used outside provider — good guard. mouseNDC design (50-52, 109): the provider stores the LIVE ref object (.current) in the context value, and setMouseNDC mutates .current.x/.y in place; this deliberately avoids re-renders on mouse move (documented in the comment). uvActive (62) is derived, true for every phase except preloading and stamp_intro. enterBuilding (66-74): sets isTransitioning true, phase 'teleporting', then after a 600ms setTimeout sets currentFloor 'lobby', phase 'lobby', isTransitioning false — comment says the camera snaps to lobby during the 600ms shred window. floorPhase (76-80): maps FloorId->BuildingPhase ('lobby'->'lobby', 'rooftop'->'rooftop', else `floor_${floor}` cast to BuildingPhase). goToFloor (82-85): immediate, NO teleport/transition — sets currentFloor and phase directly. exitFloor (87-90): sets currentFloor null and phase 'lobby' immediately. teleportTo (92-101): mirrors enterBuilding but targets arbitrary floor — isTransitioning true, phase 'teleporting', 600ms timeout then currentFloor=floor, phase=floorPhase(floor), isTransitioning false; comment explains 600ms = 300ms paper-in + 300ms hold and paper flies out on phase change. Context value (103-125) memoized; dep array (120-124) includes all callbacks and primitives. mouseNDC:mouseNDCRef.current captured — same object ref across renders, so putting the live object in the memo is fine (consumers read .x/.y at frame time).

**Issues:**
- _high_ (line 66-74,92-101): enterBuilding (69) and teleportTo (96) use setTimeout with NO cleanup. If the BuildingProvider unmounts (route change away from the landing) during the 600ms window, the timer still fires and calls setPhaseState/setCurrentFloor/setTransitioning on an unmounted provider — React will warn and the work is wasted. The timeout IDs are never stored or cleared. There is also no guard against double-invocation (rapid clicks queue multiple overlapping 600ms timers, each flipping isTransitioning false at the end and racing the phase).
- _medium_ (line 25,109): mouseNDC is exposed as a live mutable ref object but typed in the interface as a plain `{ x: number; y: number }` (line 25). Consumers cannot tell from the type that it is mutated in place and that reading it during render is unsafe/stale. The non-reactive contract is only documented in a comment (50-51), not encoded in the type. A consumer that destructures mouseNDC at render time will capture stale values.
- _medium_ (line 76-80): floorPhase builds the phase string via template literal and casts `as BuildingPhase` (line 79). No runtime validation, so passing a FloorId with no matching floor_<id> member (or a future FloorId addition) produces an invalid phase string silently typed as valid. The union and FloorId list must be kept manually in sync; 'lobby'/'rooftop' are special-cased but any new floor relies on the cast being correct.
- _low_ (line 82-101): Navigation paths are inconsistent: enterBuilding and teleportTo animate through 'teleporting' (600ms), but goToFloor and exitFloor switch phase instantly with no transition and no isTransitioning flag. Callers must know which to use to get the shred animation; exitFloor jumps to lobby with no visual transition, which looks abrupt vs teleportTo.
- _low_ (line 73,100): The 600ms transition duration is a magic number duplicated in enterBuilding and teleportTo and must match the PaperShredTransition animation timing (300+300 per the comment) defined in a different file. No shared constant, so the timer and the CSS/animation can drift out of sync.
- _nit_ (line 54,69-72,96-98): setPhase is exported but is just setPhaseState renamed; meanwhile higher-level actions (enterBuilding etc.) bypass it and call setPhaseState directly. Mixed usage makes it harder to add cross-cutting logging/guards in one place.

**Dead code / TODOs:** No TODOs. setPhase is a thin wrapper over setPhaseState that adds nothing (could be the raw setter) — minor redundancy, not dead.

### `frontend/src/immersive/canvas/lobby/LobbyRoom.tsx`  _(404 lines)_

**Purpose:** Procedurally builds the entire ornate lobby interior in Three.js: marble checkerboard floor, paneled walls with gold wainscot/cap/base/crown trim, coffered ceiling with beam grid, 8-arm chandelier with per-pendant point lights, four archival pillars, four wall sconces, an entry arch threshold, a reception desk, the Living Ledger pedestal, and interior fill lighting.
**Exports:** `LobbyRoom (default)`
**Props/inputs:** LobbyRoom: no props. LivingLedger is invoked here with position={[0,0,LOBBY_Z-3]}. Sub-components (LobbyFloor, LobbyWalls, CofferedCeiling, Chandelier, ReceptionDesk, LobbyPillars, WallSconces, EntryThreshold) take no props.
**State/refs:** No React state. useMemo: LobbyFloor.tiles (tile layout array, keyed on cols/rows) and two tile materials (lightTileMat/darkTileMat). Module-level shared materials (floorMat, wallMat, trimMat, stoneMat, darkMat) created once at import. No refs, no useFrame here (all geometry static; animation lives in LivingLedger).
**Hooks:** `useMemo (tiles array, lightTileMat, darkTileMat)`
**Dependencies:** `react (useMemo)`, `three (THREE)`, `./LivingLedger`
**Data flow:** No data. Entirely declarative geometry placed from module constants (LOBBY_X/Y/Z, ROOM_W/H/D) and local layout math. Renders LivingLedger which is the only data-driven element (live texture).
**Styling:** Three.js MeshStandardMaterial only. Five shared module-scope materials reused via <primitive object={mat}>: floorMat (#e8e0cc), wallMat (#f0e8d8), trimMat gold (#a8842c metalness .62), stoneMat (#d4ccb8), darkMat (#1e1a12). Plus inline per-mesh materials for tiles, ceiling coffers, desk body, chandelier/sconce bulbs (emissive gold). Palette is warm paper/gold/stone, consistent with the Forensic Ledger identity.

**Details:** Layout constants (8-13): LOBBY_X=0, LOBBY_Y=-9.5 (matches BuildingScene ground/floor level), LOBBY_Z=-30 (room center), ROOM_W=9.8, ROOM_H=9, ROOM_D=24. Comment block (5-7) records building shell BW=11/BH=20/BD=5 centered at z=-22, interior z≈-19.5..-47. Shared materials (16-30) instantiated once at module load — efficient reuse but global mutable singletons (see issues). LobbyFloor (33-76): TILE_SIZE=1.6; cols=floor(9.8/1.6)=6, rows=floor(24/1.6)=15 -> 90 tile meshes; centered checkerboard via (r+c)%2; each tile is a planeGeometry (TILE_SIZE-0.02) flat at y=LOBBY_Y+0.005 (5mm above grout base to avoid z-fight), receiveShadow; a full-room grout base plane at LOBBY_Y uses shared floorMat. tiles useMemo deps [cols,rows]. Tile materials memoized separately. LobbyWalls (79-143): WAINSCOT_H=1.4; back wall (9.8 x 9 x 0.28 at z-12), left/right walls (0.28 x 9 x 24), ceiling slab (9.8 x 0.28 x 24 stoneMat). Wainscot: for each side wall x (±ROOM_W/2 offset 0.15) builds 6 panels (pWidth=ROOM_D/6-0.08); gold cap rail and base rail full-length boxes; crown moulding boxes. CofferedCeiling (146-190): COLS=4 ROWS=8 -> 32 coffer recess boxes at ceiling height, plus beam grid of ROWS+1=9 horizontal and COLS+1=5 vertical beams in trim gold. Chandelier (193-247): hung at CY=LOBBY_Y+ROOM_H-0.6, centered z=LOBBY_Z+2; central boss sphere (emissive gold), drop rod cylinder, 8 arms on radius-1.4 circle each with arm cylinder, emissive pendant bulb (emissiveIntensity 2.5), AND a pointLight per pendant (intensity 0.6 distance 6) — 8 dynamic point lights from chandelier alone. Arm rotation uses [0,-angle,Math.atan2(0.1,r)] (the atan2(0.1,1.4)≈0.071rad tilt is a constant droop). ReceptionDesk (250-275): desk body box, stone top, gold front panel, dark 'VERITA lettering' slab (just a box, not real text). LobbyPillars (278-309): PILLAR_H=ROOM_H-0.4=8.6; four pillars (two pairs at z=LOBBY_Z-ROOM_D/2+4 and z=LOBBY_Z), each tapered shaft cylinder (12 seg), gold capital, dark base plinth. WallSconces (312-347): four from [x,y,z,ry] tuple array at y=LOBBY_Y+3.8, backplate/arm/emissive bulb plus a pointLight each (intensity 0.9 distance 5) — 4 more dynamic lights. EntryThreshold (350-375): arch frame, two piers, gold arch band at front opening (z=LOBBY_Z+ROOM_D/2-0.5). LobbyRoom (378-404) composes all plus LivingLedger at [0,0,LOBBY_Z-3], interior ambientLight (#f0e8d8, 0.65) and ceiling fill pointLight (intensity 1.8 distance 18 decay 1.5). Total dynamic point lights the lobby mounts: 8 chandelier + 4 sconce + 1 ceiling fill + 1 LivingLedger PedestalGlow = 14, on top of BuildingScene's 4 exterior lights.

**Issues:**
- _high_ (line 16-30): Module-scope shared materials floorMat/wallMat/trimMat/stoneMat/darkMat (lines 16-30) are created at import time and never disposed; they persist for the app lifetime even when the lobby unmounts (LobbySceneContents conditionally renders LobbyRoom). Sharing one material instance across dozens of <primitive object={mat}> meshes means r3f will NOT dispose them on unmount (not created by JSX) — combined with module scope this is permanent retention; a real leak under hot-reload or scene re-creation.
- _high_ (line 234-241,342,395-401): Very high dynamic-light count: the lobby mounts 14 point lights (8 chandelier pendants + 4 sconces + ceiling fill + pedestal glow) plus the exterior rig's 4 lights and 2 shadow-casting directional maps. Real-time per-pixel lighting with this many lights on meshStandardMaterial is a serious performance trap on mid/low GPUs and mobile, especially with shadows enabled. No light-count budget, baking, or LOD. Consider baking the chandelier/sconce glow into emissive + a couple of representative lights.
- _medium_ (line 62-68): LobbyFloor renders 90 individual tile meshes (6x15) as separate draw calls (lines 63-68) instead of InstancedMesh or a single texture. 90 draw calls just for the checkerboard. Wainscot (12), coffers (32), beams (14), pillars (12 meshes) etc. push the lobby into hundreds of un-instanced draw calls.
- _medium_ (line 34,38-50): useMemo dependency for tiles is [cols,rows] (line 50) but TILE_SIZE is a local const inside LobbyFloor (line 34) re-declared every render and not in deps — currently fine because it is constant, but if TILE_SIZE ever became dynamic the memo would go stale. The memo also closes over LOBBY_Z/ROOM_D (module constants, fine).
- _low_ (line 105-117,151-167): Nested Array.from(...).map(...) returning arrays of meshes inside JSX (e.g. wainscot 105-117) relies on React flattening nested arrays; keys are unique but the pattern is fragile/hard to read. CofferedCeiling wraps each coffer in an extra <group> (153) with a key but no transform — 32 unnecessary empty group nodes.
- _low_ (line 8): LOBBY_X constant (line 8) is declared but never referenced — dead constant.
- _low_ (line 268-272): The 'VERITA' branding on the reception desk (268-272) is a solid box, not text geometry, so it renders as a black rectangle, not the brand wordmark. Real Text/TextGeometry or a texture is needed — current state is a placeholder.
- _nit_ (line 227-232,336-340): High emissiveIntensity values (chandelier pendants 2.5, sconces 3) combined with ACES + 14 point lights may clip; no bloom postprocessing is wired here so the emissive reads as bright dots without glow — the 'lights' have no postFX.
- _nit_ (line 34,80,147,209,219): Magic numbers throughout (tile 1.6, wainscot 1.4, panel counts 6, coffer 4x8, pillar taper 0.28/0.32, chandelier radius 1.4, atan2(0.1,r) droop) with no named constants beyond room dimensions — intentional for hand-authored scene but tuning-error-prone.

**Dead code / TODOs:** The 'VERITA lettering' (269-272) is a plain dark box, not real text geometry — reads as a black bar, not letters; an unmarked placeholder for branding. LOBBY_X (line 8) is defined but never used.

### `frontend/src/immersive/canvas/lobby/LivingLedger.tsx`  _(138 lines)_

**Purpose:** Renders the centerpiece 'Living Ledger' — a floating open book on a gold-ringed pedestal whose left page displays a live, continuously-updating canvas texture (from useLiveLedger) representing the running compliance ledger. Includes an idle float animation and a pulsing pedestal glow light.
**Exports:** `LivingLedger (default)`
**Props/inputs:** LivingLedger: { position?: [number, number, number] } defaulting to [0,0,0] (called from LobbyRoom with [0,0,LOBBY_Z-3]). PedestalGlow and BookSpine take no props.
**State/refs:** No React state. Refs: PedestalGlow.lightRef (THREE.PointLight), LivingLedger.bookRef (THREE.Group). texture comes from useLiveLedger. useMemo: coverMat, pageMat.
**Hooks:** `useRef (lightRef, bookRef)`, `useMemo (coverMat, pageMat)`, `useFrame (PedestalGlow pulse; book idle float/rotation)`, `useLiveLedger (custom hook returning THREE.Texture | null)`
**Dependencies:** `react (useRef, useMemo)`, `@react-three/fiber (useFrame)`, `three (THREE)`, `../../hooks/useLiveLedger`
**Data flow:** The only data-driven element in this subsystem. useLiveLedger returns a live THREE.Texture (a CanvasTexture repeatedly redrawn, representing 'live' ledger entries) mapped onto the left page material. Texture source/update cadence lives in useLiveLedger (outside these five files). All geometry/positions hardcoded. No props beyond placement.
**Styling:** Three.js MeshStandardMaterial. Pedestal/cover dark brown (#2a2318 / #1a1308), gold ring + corner brackets + spine emboss (#a8842c metalness ~0.7, slight emissive), page material uses live texture (color white when textured, cream #f5efdf fallback) with subtle emissive (#ffefc8, intensity 0.04 when textured else 0). Warm gold/paper palette.

**Details:** Dimensions (7-10): PAGE_W=2.1, PAGE_H=2.7, PAGE_D=0.04, PEDESTAL_H=1.4. PedestalGlow (13-29): pointLight at [0,PEDESTAL_H+0.6,0] whose intensity is animated each frame to 1.2 + sin(clock.elapsedTime*1.4)*0.35 (oscillates ~0.85..1.55) — gentle breathing glow, null-guarded. BookSpine (32-39): thin box behind the pages. LivingLedger (42-137): pulls texture = useLiveLedger(); bookRef on the floating book group. useFrame idle animation (47-51): book group y = sin(t*0.6)*0.025 (±2.5cm float) and rotation.y = sin(t*0.3)*0.03 (±0.03rad sway), null-guarded. coverMat (54-57) memoized static dark material (comment calls it the 'fallback blank material when texture not yet ready' but it is actually used for the RIGHT page, see issues). pageMat (59-70) memoized on [texture]: map=texture??undefined, color #ffffff if textured else #f5efdf, emissive #ffefc8 intensity 0.04 when textured else 0 — material recreated whenever texture reference changes. JSX (72-136): pedestal cylinder (r 0.55 top / 0.65 bottom, h 1.4, 16 seg, cast+receive shadow), gold plinth ring on top, <PedestalGlow/>, then the floating <group ref=bookRef> containing: left page box (PAGE_W x PAGE_H x PAGE_D) at x=-PAGE_W/2-0.02 with the live pageMat (castShadow), right page box at x=+PAGE_W/2+0.02 rotated y -0.08 with coverMat, four gold corner brackets via nested [-1,1]x[-1,1] map, the BookSpine, and a 'VERITA embossed label on spine' thin gold box. Idle float and glow are the only animations; live content updates come from the texture itself.

**Issues:**
- _high_ (line 59-70): pageMat is recreated via useMemo whenever `texture` changes (lines 59-70), but the OLD MeshStandardMaterial is never disposed. If useLiveLedger swaps the texture reference (or the material is recreated), each previous material instance leaks GPU resources. Likewise if the live texture is a CanvasTexture being recreated rather than updated-in-place (texture.needsUpdate=true), the old textures leak. Add disposal on material/texture replacement.
- _medium_ (line 106-120): The four gold corner brackets are positioned with x = PAGE_W + 0.02 + sx*(PAGE_W/2 - 0.12) (line 111). With PAGE_W=2.1 the base offset is ~2.12, so brackets sit at x≈1.07 and x≈3.17 — centered near the RIGHT page (at x≈1.07) but the right bracket at x≈3.17 is far outside the page edge (right page right edge ≈2.12). The math double-counts PAGE_W and likely renders one bracket floating off the book — looks like a positioning bug.
- _medium_ (line 43,59-70): useLiveLedger is called but its return is only consumed for the page map; there is no loading/error state and no Suspense — if the hook throws or the canvas/texture fails, the page silently falls back to a blank cream material with no indication. For a 'living' centerpiece, a stuck/blank ledger is a silent failure.
- _low_ (line 47-51): Idle-float useFrame sets bookRef.current.position.y to an absolute sin value (line 49) rather than relative to a base position. Fine now because the book group's base y is 0, but any future base-y offset on the inner group would be overwritten every frame — the animation owns the absolute y, which is brittle.
- _low_ (line 53): Misleading stale comment at line 53 claims coverMat is the texture-not-ready fallback; it is actually the right-page cover material used unconditionally.
- _low_ (line 15-18,47-51): No prefers-reduced-motion handling for the continuous idle float (book bob/sway) or the pulsing pedestal light. Against the project's WCAG AAA goal, perpetual decorative motion with no reduced-motion opt-out is an accessibility concern.
- _nit_ (line 124-134): VERITA embossed label on spine (124-134) and corner brackets are again plain boxes, not actual lettering — consistent with LobbyRoom's placeholder approach but not literal branding text.
- _nit_ (line 131): Inline new THREE.Color in render-path material props (e.g. line 131) allocates per render of that mesh — negligible here but a pattern to watch; the ones inside useMemo are fine.

**Dead code / TODOs:** Comment on line 53 ('Fallback blank material when texture not yet ready') is misleading/stale — coverMat is the permanent RIGHT-page cover material, not a swapped-out fallback; the actual texture-not-ready fallback is handled inside pageMat's color/emissive ternaries.

---

## Immersive exterior 3D geometry + UV-reveal shader material

> This subsystem renders the static art-deco "Verita" building exterior and its interactive entrance inside the react-three-fiber immersive scene. BuildingFacade.tsx is a purely declarative geometry tree (body, crown, trim, windows, portico, sign, parapet, pilasters) built from shared THREE materials and hardcoded dimension constants. EntranceDoors.tsx adds the interactive layer: two GSAP-animated swinging door panels plus a 600-particle instanced paper-scatter burst that fires on first click and triggers the building-entry transition via BuildingContext. HashChainSpine.tsx renders a vertical chain of 12 tori that animate grey→gold on mount (staggered GSAP seal) with continuous idle rotation. uvReveal.ts is a standalone GLSL ShaderMaterial factory plus canvas-texture generators implementing the "UV lamp" reveal effect (cyan hidden ink revealed within a mouse-following circle) — it is not imported by any of the three TSX files here; those use plain MeshStandardMaterial. The doors are the only file wired to live app state (BuildingContext); the rest is fully self-contained mock/decorative geometry.

### `frontend/src/immersive/canvas/exterior/BuildingFacade.tsx`  _(196 lines)_

**Purpose:** Declarative, fully static art-deco building exterior for the immersive approach scene. No interactivity, no animation, no app state — a composition of THREE meshes positioned around hardcoded building dimensions.
**Exports:** `default BuildingFacade (React component)`
**Props/inputs:** BuildingFacade takes no props. Internal helper Window takes { x: number; y: number }. ArtDecoCrown, FloorTrimBands, EntrancePortico, VeritaSignMesh take no props.
**State/refs:** No React state, no refs. The only hook usage is useMemo inside VeritaSignMesh for the canvas texture.
**Hooks:** `useMemo (VeritaSignMesh only)`
**Dependencies:** `react (useMemo)`, `three (THREE.MeshStandardMaterial, THREE.Color, THREE.CanvasTexture)`
**Data flow:** 100% self-contained/mock. All geometry derives from module-level constants BW=11, BH=20, BD=5, BZ=-22. No props, no context, no API. The VERITA sign text is rasterized in-component to a canvas (avoids a webfont fetch).
**Styling:** r3f JSX primitives (boxGeometry/planeGeometry) styled by six module-level shared MeshStandardMaterial instances (STONE, STONE_DEEP, GOLD, VERMILION, WINDOW_GLOW, WINDOW_FRAME, DARK_ENTRANCE — actually seven) attached via <primitive object={...}/>. No CSS, no design tokens; colors are raw hex literals (#f0e8d8 stone, #a8842c gold, #c2331f vermilion, etc.) matching the paper/ink/stamp brand palette.

**Details:** Module-level shared materials (lines 5-11): seven MeshStandardMaterial singletons reused across many meshes via <primitive object>. GOLD and WINDOW_GLOW carry emissive (GOLD emissiveIntensity 0.22; WINDOW_GLOW emissive #ff9e2c intensity 1.1, transparent opacity 0.9). Dimension constants (14-17): BW 11, BH 20, BD 5, BZ -22. Window() (19-39): group placed at building front face (BZ - BD/2 + 0.01); three stacked meshes — recess box 1.3x1.7x0.3 (STONE_DEEP), frame border at z+0.12 (WINDOW_FRAME), amber glass plane 0.92x1.32 at z+0.17 (WINDOW_GLOW). ArtDecoCrown() (41-69): three stepped boxes narrowing by factors 0.82 then 0.60 in width/depth at y 0.7/2.0/3.1, a 0.6-cube GOLD finial at y3.9, and a thin gold trim band at y1.4; group sits at BH/2+0.1. FloorTrimBands() (71-84): four gold bands at hardcoded ys [-5.8,-1.6,2.6,6.8], each box BW+0.14 wide. EntrancePortico() (86-125): group at y=-BH/2+3.2, z=BZ-BD/2-0.1; canopy slab + dark underside, vermilion arch face 5.8x3.8x0.22, dark arch opening, two STONE pilasters at x ±3.2, and three stacked steps generated by mapping [0,0.18,0.36] with step width 6.0 + i*0.4 and depth 0.9+dz. VeritaSignMesh() (127-150): builds a 512x128 canvas, fills #f0e8d8 paper, draws 'VERITA' in bold 88px Georgia with letterSpacing '8px', wraps in THREE.CanvasTexture (memoized once with empty deps), maps onto a 5.5x1.2 plane at y=-BH/2+7.2. Main component (152-195): main body box BW×BH×BD with castShadow+receiveShadow (the only shadow-casting mesh besides side pilasters), then crown/trim/portico, a 4×4 window grid via flatMap over x[-3.6,-1.2,1.2,3.6] × y[-5.0,-1.0,3.0,7.0], the sign, a roof parapet box, and two side pilaster strips at x ±(BW/2±0.05) with castShadow. Notable: shared materials are never disposed (module-level so they live for app lifetime — acceptable but worth noting); the VeritaSignMesh CanvasTexture is created per component mount and never disposed (small leak if facade unmounts/remounts repeatedly).

**Issues:**
- _low_ (line 140-142): VeritaSignMesh creates a THREE.CanvasTexture in useMemo but never disposes it (no useEffect cleanup calling tex.dispose()). If BuildingFacade unmounts/remounts (e.g. phase changes in/out of exterior), GPU texture memory leaks one texture per mount. Same applies to the WINDOW_GLOW/STONE module-level materials, though those are intentionally app-lifetime singletons.
- _low_ (line 5-11): Seven module-level shared MeshStandardMaterial singletons are mutable and shared across all instances. Fine while static, but any future per-mesh tweak (e.g. hover tint) mutating one of these would affect every mesh using it. Also they are created at module-eval time even if the facade never renders.
- _nit_ (line 147): VERITA sign plane material is marked transparent but the canvas is fully opaque (paper background filled). transparent:true with no alpha forces an unnecessary alpha-blend pass / can introduce z-sorting artifacts.
- _nit_ (line 73,170-175): Heavy use of unexplained magic numbers for floor-band ys ([-5.8,-1.6,2.6,6.8]) and window grid positions that are not derived from BH/floor count — brittle if BH changes. Comment says '4 columns × 4 rows' which matches but coordinates are hand-tuned literals.
- _nit_ (line 147): Inline meshStandardMaterial here (not a shared singleton) is recreated on every render of VeritaSignMesh, unlike the rest of the file which uses singletons — minor inconsistency; cheap since the component rarely re-renders.

### `frontend/src/immersive/canvas/exterior/EntranceDoors.tsx`  _(203 lines)_

**Purpose:** Interactive entrance: two GSAP-animated swinging door panels with hover affordance, a 600-instance paper-scatter particle burst, and the door frame. First door click fires the paper shred and triggers building entry through BuildingContext.
**Exports:** `default EntranceDoors (React component)`
**Props/inputs:** EntranceDoors: none. PaperScatter: { active: boolean }. DoorPanel: { side: 'left' | 'right'; onOpen: () => void }. DoorFrame: none.
**State/refs:** DoorPanel: useState hovered (bool), useRef openedRef (bool latch), useRef groupRef (THREE.Group). PaperScatter: useRef meshRef (InstancedMesh), useRef dataRef (per-particle pos/vel/rot/rotVel array), useMemo dummy (THREE.Object3D). EntranceDoors: useState scatterActive (bool), useRef openCountRef (number).
**Hooks:** `useRef`, `useState`, `useCallback`, `useMemo`, `useFrame (@react-three/fiber)`, `useThree (@react-three/fiber)`, `useBuildingContext (custom)`
**Dependencies:** `react (useRef/useState/useCallback/useMemo)`, `@react-three/fiber (useFrame, useThree)`, `three`, `gsap`, `../../context/BuildingContext (useBuildingContext -> enterBuilding)`
**Data flow:** Wired to live app state via useBuildingContext(): pulls enterBuilding() and calls it 320ms after first door open. This is the only file in the subsystem connected to context. Particle physics is purely procedural/mock (Math.random seeded each mount). Geometry from hardcoded constants DOOR_W/H/THICKNESS/Z/Y.
**Styling:** r3f JSX with per-mesh MeshStandardMaterial. Door panel material is memoized on hovered (color #d4c8a8 default, #e8dcc8 hovered). Detail panels and gold handle use inline materials. Cursor is set imperatively via gl.domElement.style.cursor in a useFrame loop.

**Details:** Constants (7-11): DOOR_W 1.85, DOOR_H 3.1, DOOR_THICKNESS 0.12, DOOR_Z -17.35, DOOR_Y -8.3. PARTICLE_COUNT 600 (14). PaperScatter (16-78): dataRef initialized via useMemo([]) building 600 particles — pos spread x±1.8, y from DOOR_Y+DOOR_H/2 upward, z around DOOR_Z±1; vel x±3, y up 1-5, z forward 1-6; random Euler rot and rotVel ±2. useFrame (45-62): early-returns if !meshRef.current OR !active; applies GRAVITY=-4.5 to vel.y, integrates pos by vel*delta, integrates each rotation axis, copies into the shared dummy Object3D, scales by max(0, 1 - max(0, -p.pos.z + DOOR_Z)*0.1) (a depth-based shrink, see issues), updateMatrix, setMatrixAt(i). Sets instanceMatrix.needsUpdate. Returns null when !active (so InstancedMesh only mounts after first open). DoorPanel (81-153): isLeft → pivotX -DOOR_W, panelOffsetX +DOOR_W/2, openAngle -PI/2 (right mirrors). handleClick (98-107): guards on openedRef latch + groupRef, sets latch, calls onOpen(), GSAP rotates group.rotation.y to openAngle over 0.85s power2.inOut. useFrame (109-111): every frame sets gl.domElement.style.cursor = (hovered && !opened) ? 'pointer' : 'none' — NOTE default cursor is 'none' not 'auto/default'. Material memoized on hovered (113-121). Pivot group at hinge edge, panel mesh offset to fill opening, with onPointerOver/Out/Click + castShadow; two recessed detail panels mapped from [-0.5,0.5]; gold cylinder handle. DoorFrame (156-177): non-memoized material created fresh each render (fine, rarely re-renders); left/right jambs + top lintel. EntranceDoors (180-202): openCountRef counts opens; handleDoorOpen sets scatterActive true and, on the first open only, setTimeout(enterBuilding, 320). Renders frame, both panels, and PaperScatter. Edge cases / magic numbers: 320ms entry delay vs 850ms door swing (entry fires mid-swing, intentional 'paper covers transition'); GRAVITY -4.5; the scatter never stops or recycles — particles fly off forever while active and the loop keeps integrating 600 particles every frame indefinitely.

**Issues:**
- _high_ (line 110): Cursor side-effect runs in a per-frame useFrame and is GLOBAL on the canvas: every DoorPanel writes gl.domElement.style.cursor every frame, and both panels default to 'none'. So whenever neither door is hovered the canvas cursor is permanently hidden ('none'), and the two panels race each frame to set the cursor. This both hides the cursor unexpectedly across the whole scene and is an a11y/usability problem (invisible pointer). Setting cursor every frame is also wasteful; should be event-driven (onPointerOver/Out) and restore to 'auto'/'default', not 'none'.
- _high_ (line 45-62): PaperScatter useFrame never deactivates or recycles. Once scatterActive is true it stays true forever (no reset), so 600 particles are integrated every frame for the life of the scene even after they've flown far off-screen. Perf trap / unbounded animation with no end condition or cleanup.
- _medium_ (line 24-43): Particle init uses useMemo (not useEffect) for a side-effecting assignment to dataRef.current. Works, but conceptually a side effect in render; under React 18 StrictMode double-invoke it runs twice (harmless here, just regenerates). Reading dataRef before this useMemo runs is impossible since it's same render, but the pattern is fragile.
- _medium_ (line 189-191): Entry is gated on openCountRef.current === 1, but handleDoorOpen is also called by the second door. If the user clicks both doors quickly the second open still flips scatterActive (already true) — harmless — but there is no guard preventing enterBuilding's transition from racing with a second GSAP swing. More importantly there is no way to re-arm after entry; minor since the component unmounts on phase change.
- _medium_ (line 57): Scale formula `Math.max(0, 1 - Math.max(0, -p.pos.z + DOOR_Z) * 0.1)` is almost certainly wrong. Particles start at z≈DOOR_Z and have positive z velocity (toward camera), so p.pos.z grows > DOOR_Z, making `-p.pos.z + DOOR_Z` negative, clamped to 0 → scale stays 1 (never shrinks). The intended depth-fade likely never triggers; this is dead/ineffective logic.
- _low_ (line 102-106): GSAP tween on groupRef.current.rotation is not killed on unmount. If the component unmounts mid-swing (phase transition to teleporting at 320ms, well before 850ms swing completes) GSAP keeps animating a detached object — minor leak/no crash, but no gsap.killTweensOf cleanup in any effect.
- _low_ (line 67): instancedMesh args={[undefined, undefined, PARTICLE_COUNT]} relies on r3f to inject geometry/material from children; passing undefined for geometry/material is the documented pattern but TS-loose. Fine functionally.
- _low_ (line 157): DoorFrame creates a new MeshStandardMaterial on every render outside any memo. Rarely re-renders so cheap, but inconsistent with DoorPanel's memoized material and not disposed.
- _nit_ (line 147): Handle horizontal position uses isLeft ? +0.38 : -0.38 of DOOR_W from panelOffsetX — handles end up toward the meeting edge; correct, but the ternary plus panelOffsetX (already side-dependent) is hard to read.

### `frontend/src/immersive/canvas/exterior/HashChainSpine.tsx`  _(117 lines)_

**Purpose:** Decorative vertical 'hash chain' of 12 torus links running up the right edge of the building. On mount each link animates grey→gold (staggered GSAP 'seal') with a scale pop, and all links rotate slowly forever. Visual metaphor for the cryptographic ledger chain.
**Exports:** `default HashChainSpine (React component)`
**Props/inputs:** HashChainSpine: none. ChainLink: { index: number; position: [number, number, number] }. LinkConnector: { y: number }.
**State/refs:** No React state. ChainLink uses two refs: meshRef (THREE.Mesh) and matRef (THREE.MeshStandardMaterial).
**Hooks:** `useRef`, `useEffect (ChainLink seal animation)`, `useFrame (ChainLink idle rotation)`
**Dependencies:** `react (useRef/useEffect)`, `@react-three/fiber (useFrame)`, `three (THREE.Color)`, `gsap`
**Data flow:** Fully self-contained/decorative. No props from app, no context, no API. All positions derived from constants LINK_COUNT 12, SPINE_X 6.4, SPINE_Z -22, SPINE_Y_BOTTOM -9, LINK_SPACING 1.8. The 'seal' animation is purely time/index driven, not data-driven (does not reflect any real chain state).
**Styling:** r3f JSX. ChainLink uses an inline MeshStandardMaterial (color #6a6050 grey, metalness 0.65, roughness 0.35, emissive black, intensity 0) animated by GSAP. LinkConnector uses inline material color #8a7840. Gold target color #a8842c is expressed as raw normalized RGB (0.658/0.518/0.173) in the tween.

**Details:** Constants (6-10): LINK_COUNT 12, SPINE_X 6.4, SPINE_Z -22, SPINE_Y_BOTTOM -9, LINK_SPACING 1.8. ChainLink (13-86): useEffect (23-64) keyed on [index] runs once per mount — computes delay = 0.6 + index*0.22 (bottom links seal first), then GSAP tweens matRef emissiveIntensity → 0.55 over 0.6s at that delay; in onStart it kicks two more tweens animating material.color and material.emissive RGB toward gold (0.658,0.518,0.173) over 0.5s. Separately a scale pop via gsap.fromTo on meshRef.scale 1→1.18 over 0.14s, delay+0.05, yoyo+repeat:1 (pops and returns). useFrame (67-71) adds continuous z-rotation delta*(0.3 + index*0.04) so higher links spin faster. torusGeometry args [0.3, 0.075, 8, 20] (radius, tube, radial seg, tubular seg). Composition (98-116): maps LINK_COUNT, y = SPINE_Y_BOTTOM + i*LINK_SPACING, alternating z offset ±0.12 (isAlternate i%2===1) to give a woven look; a LinkConnector cylinder is placed between consecutive links (skipped for last) at y + LINK_SPACING/2. Magic numbers: seal base delay 0.6 + 0.22/link (~3.0s total for 12 links), pop 1.18 scale, rotation base 0.3 rad/s.

**Issues:**
- _high_ (line 23-64): Multiple GSAP tweens (emissiveIntensity, color, emissive, scale pop) are started on mount but never killed. No useEffect cleanup returning gsap.killTweensOf(matRef.current)/(meshRef.current). If HashChainSpine unmounts during the ~3s seal sequence (likely on the exterior→teleporting phase change), GSAP continues mutating detached THREE objects. Memory/CPU leak, and on remount the staggered tweens restart with no guarantee the previous ones stopped.
- _medium_ (line 67-71): Idle rotation runs forever via useFrame with no pause/visibility gating. 12 links rotating + the seal tweens keep the scene's render loop busy even when the building exterior is off-screen (after entering the building) unless the parent unmounts it. Verify the parent actually unmounts on phase change; otherwise this is wasted GPU each frame.
- _medium_ (line 29-47): Nested GSAP tweens (color/emissive tweens started inside the onStart of the emissiveIntensity tween) create implicit timing coupling and three concurrent tweens per link. The inner color tween has delay:0 inside onStart so effectively starts at the same `delay` — the nesting is unnecessary indirection and makes the timeline hard to reason about / kill.
- _low_ (line 37-39): Gold color is hardcoded as raw normalized RGB floats (0.658, 0.518, 0.173) with a comment '#a8842c'. Brittle and duplicated from the GOLD material in BuildingFacade; if the brand gold changes these floats silently drift. Should derive from a shared THREE.Color('#a8842c').toArray() or token.
- _low_ (line 75): torusGeometry uses low tessellation (radialSegments 8) so links look faceted; intentional perhaps, but each of 12 links creates its own geometry instance (no sharing) — could be instanced/shared for perf.
- _nit_ (line 89-96): LinkConnector material and torus materials are all created inline per render; geometries/materials are not disposed. Acceptable for a small static set but inconsistent with disposal hygiene.

### `frontend/src/immersive/canvas/materials/uvReveal.ts`  _(145 lines)_

**Purpose:** Standalone GLSL ShaderMaterial factory and canvas-texture generators for the 'UV lamp' reveal effect: hidden cyan ink (fingerprints, risk readouts, evidence boxes, VERITA watermark) is revealed only inside a soft, flickering circle that follows the mouse in NDC space.
**Exports:** `UV_VERT (GLSL string)`, `UV_FRAG (GLSL string)`, `createUVRevealMaterial(baseMap, uvMap) -> THREE.ShaderMaterial`, `makeUVInkTex(width, height, drawFn) -> THREE.CanvasTexture`, `drawRiskLabUVInk(ctx, w, h) -> void`
**Props/inputs:** N/A (pure module, no React). Function params: createUVRevealMaterial(baseMap: THREE.Texture, uvMap: THREE.Texture); makeUVInkTex(width:number, height:number, drawFn:(ctx,w,h)=>void); drawRiskLabUVInk(ctx: CanvasRenderingContext2D, w:number, h:number).
**State/refs:** N/A. The shader holds mutable uniforms: uBaseMap, uUVMap, uMouse (Vector2), uRadius (0.40), uTime (0). The caller is expected to update uMouse and uTime each frame (no animation loop lives here).
**Hooks:** `none — not a React module`
**Dependencies:** `three (THREE.ShaderMaterial, THREE.Texture, THREE.Vector2, THREE.CanvasTexture, THREE.RepeatWrapping)`
**Data flow:** Self-contained. drawRiskLabUVInk paints HARDCODED MOCK forensic content: 'RISK SCORE: 0.847', 'FRAUD PROBABILITY: 94.2%', 'ANOMALY CLUSTER: K=7', 'CHAIN HEAD: 0xA8F2C3D1' — none of these come from the API/model; they are decorative fixed strings. Probability-dot grid uses Math.random() at draw time (non-deterministic per render). uMouse must be fed from BuildingContext.mouseNDC by whatever component uses this material (not done within any of the other three exterior files — this module is unused by them).
**Styling:** GLSL fragment shader. Reveal = outer*0.65 + inner*0.35 where outer = 1 - smoothstep(0, uRadius, dist) and inner = 1 - smoothstep(0, uRadius*0.35, dist), giving a soft circle with a hot core. Flicker multiplies reveal by 0.88 + 0.12*sin(uTime*14.3 + ndc.x*8.0). UV ink composited over base via a screen blend mixed by reveal. Canvas ink is cyan (#7df9ff) on black (#000000) background.

**Details:** Vertex shader (4-14): passes vUv and the clip-space position vClip to the fragment; gl_Position = projection*modelView*position. Fragment (16-46): computes ndc = vClip.xy/vClip.w, dist = distance(ndc, uMouse) — so the reveal circle is in normalized device coords and follows the mouse uniform; uRadius default 0.40. Composites base*uvInk via screen blend then mixes by `reveal * uvInk.a + uvInk.r * reveal*0.5` (note: uses uvInk.r as a secondary mask since ink is cyan/high-blue not high-red — see issues). createUVRevealMaterial (49-64): builds ShaderMaterial; does NOT set transparent, depthWrite/Test, or blending — opaque by default, alpha forced to 1.0 in frag. makeUVInkTex (67-81): creates an offscreen canvas, fills black, runs drawFn, returns a CanvasTexture with wrapS/wrapT = RepeatWrapping (no colorSpace set; for an authored ink map sRGB vs linear could matter). drawRiskLabUVInk (84-144): four fingerprint glyphs at fractional coords, each drawn as concentric arcs r 6→28 step 5 with fading cyan alpha (0.55 - r*0.012) plus 8 radial ridge lines; mono text annotations (hardcoded values); two evidence-box strokeRects with 'EVIDENCE' labels; a 6×10 probability dot grid where dots only draw if Math.random() > 0.6 with radius 3+prob*3; and a 12%-alpha 80px Georgia 'VERITA' watermark centered (uses ctx.save/restore around globalAlpha). Magic numbers: flicker freq 14.3 & spatial term 8.0, inner-core factor 0.35, blend weights 0.65/0.35 and 0.5, radius 0.40.

**Issues:**
- _high_ (line 27-28): Reveal distance is computed in clip/NDC space (ndc = vClip.xy/vClip.w) and compared against uMouse. NDC is aspect-ratio-unaware, so the reveal 'circle' is actually an ellipse stretched by the viewport aspect ratio (wider on widescreen). Needs aspect correction (multiply x by aspect, or pass resolution uniform) to be a true circle.
- _medium_ (line 44): Final mix factor `reveal * uvInk.a + uvInk.r * reveal * 0.5` relies on uvInk.r as a mask, but the ink is cyan #7df9ff (r≈0.49) on black — red channel is mid-level for ink and 0 for background, which roughly works, but it's an accidental coupling: any future non-cyan ink (e.g. pure blue or green) would have low/zero red and silently fail to reveal. Mask should use luminance or alpha explicitly.
- _medium_ (line 127): drawRiskLabUVInk's probability dot grid calls Math.random() at texture-build time, so the 'forensic' dots are non-deterministic and change every time the texture is regenerated (e.g. on remount/StrictMode double-render). Decorative, but means the hidden content is not reproducible and double-renders differ.
- _medium_ (line 53-63): ShaderMaterial sets no transparent/blending/depth config and the fragment always outputs alpha 1.0. If this material is meant to overlay a base document where the un-revealed areas should show the base unchanged, that works (it samples uBaseMap), but it cannot be layered as a transparent decal over other geometry — the design intent (decal vs full surface) is ambiguous and undocumented.
- _low_ (line 108,110): Fonts reference 'JetBrains Mono' / Georgia by name with no font-load guarantee. If the webfont isn't loaded when the canvas is rasterized, the browser silently falls back to a default mono/serif, changing the hidden-ink appearance. No document.fonts.ready await before drawing.
- _low_ (line 78-80): makeUVInkTex sets RepeatWrapping but never sets needsUpdate (CanvasTexture sets it on construct, OK) and does not set t.colorSpace. For authored color content THREE r152+ defaults textures to no/linear handling depending on usage; cyan may render slightly off without SRGBColorSpace. Also the returned texture is never disposed by this helper (caller's responsibility, undocumented).
- _low_ (line 84-144,108-114): Hardcoded mock forensic data ('RISK SCORE: 0.847', 'FRAUD PROBABILITY: 94.2%', 'ANOMALY CLUSTER: K=7', 'CHAIN HEAD: 0xA8F2C3D1'). These look like real model/chain outputs but are fixed strings — a reviewer/screenshot could mistake them for live data. Worth a comment marking them as decorative, or wiring to real values.
- _nit_ (line 49-64): createUVRevealMaterial provides no way to update uMouse/uTime and no glslVersion; consumers must reach into material.uniforms directly each frame. A thin helper or typed uniforms object would reduce stringly-typed access. Also UV_VERT/UV_FRAG are exported but the whole module is not imported by the three exterior TSX files reviewed — appears unused by this subsystem (potential dead code or used elsewhere).

---

## Immersive 3D Building — Floor Scenes (immersive/canvas/floors)

> A react-three-fiber subsystem that renders five themed 3D "rooms" stacked vertically inside an immersive scrollable building. FloorScene.tsx is the dispatcher: it reads the current `phase` from BuildingContext and lazy-loads exactly one floor component. Each floor (Studio, RiskLab, Detective, Vault, Rooftop) is a self-contained `<group>` of declarative meshes/lights themed around Verita's "Forensic Ledger" identity (blueprints, UV evidence, noir detective, sealed archive vault, night rooftop). All floors share the same canonical room dimensions (RW 9.8 x RH 9 x RD 24, centered at RZ -30) and differ only by their floor-base Y (STUDIO_FY 2.5, RISK_FY 14.5, DETECTIVE_FY 26.5, VAULT_FY 38.5, ROOFTOP_FY 50.5 — a 12-unit step). Geometry is 100% procedural/mock: there is no API or props data; textures are drawn on offscreen canvases at module/render time, and all labels/values (SHAP scores, risk score 0.847, chain hash, archive codes) are hardcoded decoration. Only RiskLabFloor consumes live state, via the useRiskLabUVReveal shader hook which reads mouse NDC from context.

### `frontend/src/immersive/canvas/floors/FloorScene.tsx`  _(28 lines)_

**Purpose:** Phase-driven dispatcher that selects and lazy-renders exactly one floor component based on BuildingContext.phase. Acts as the code-split boundary so only the active floor's geometry/textures load.
**Exports:** `default FloorScene (React component)`
**Props/inputs:** none
**State/refs:** none
**Hooks:** `useBuildingContext (custom context hook, line 11)`
**Dependencies:** `react (lazy, Suspense)`, `../../context/BuildingContext (useBuildingContext)`, `./StudioFloor`, `./RiskLabFloor`, `./DetectiveFloor`, `./VaultFloor`, `./RooftopFloor (all via React.lazy)`
**Data flow:** Reads `phase` (a BuildingPhase string union) from context only. No props, no API. Maps phase->component and renders. Floor components themselves take no props and produce mock geometry.
**Styling:** None (3D component tree only). Visual styling lives entirely in the child floor meshes/materials.

**Details:** Lines 4-8 declare five lazy() imports. Lines 13-19 are a ternary chain mapping phase values: 'floor_studio'->StudioFloor, 'floor_risk'->RiskLabFloor, 'floor_detective'->DetectiveFloor, 'floor_vault'->VaultFloor, 'rooftop'->RooftopFloor, else null. Line 21 short-circuits to null if no match (e.g. phases 'preloading','stamp_intro','lobby' all render nothing here). Lines 23-25 wrap the chosen Floor in <Suspense fallback={null}> so the chunk loads silently. BuildingPhase (confirmed in BuildingContext.tsx lines 11-15) includes the five matched phases plus 'preloading'/'stamp_intro'/etc which intentionally fall through to null. Because each floor is a separate lazy chunk, switching phases unmounts the prior floor and mounts a new one — there is no cross-fade or keep-alive, so all per-floor canvas textures are regenerated on every (re)mount (see per-floor issues). Suspense fallback is null, meaning during chunk load the scene shows empty space with no placeholder.

**Issues:**
- _low_ (line 23): Suspense fallback is null — while a floor chunk downloads the room is empty (no loading mesh/spinner). Acceptable for small chunks but a visible gap on slow networks.
- _nit_ (line 13-19): Phase->component mapping is a hand-written ternary chain; a lookup object (Record<BuildingPhase, LazyComponent>) would be more maintainable and type-checked exhaustively against the union.

### `frontend/src/immersive/canvas/floors/StudioFloor.tsx`  _(248 lines)_

**Purpose:** Themed 3D 'design studio / drafting' room: blueprint-grid floor, archival blue-cream walls, three tilted drafting tables with schematic paper, ceiling track lighting, wall document racks, and a large schematic poster. Represents the analysis/authoring workspace.
**Exports:** `STUDIO_FY (const = 2.5, floor base Y)`, `default StudioFloor (component)`
**Props/inputs:** StudioFloor: none. Internal subcomponents: DraftingTable({position:[n,n,n], rotY?=0, schematicIdx}), TrackLighting({y,z}), DocumentRack({x,z}).
**State/refs:** No React state. Refs: imports useRef (line 1) but it is UNUSED in this file (no ref declared). No useFrame/animation — this floor is fully static.
**Hooks:** `useMemo (makeBlueprintTex at line 169; makeSchematicTex per DraftingTable at line 73)`, `useRef imported but unused`, `useFrame imported but unused`
**Dependencies:** `react (useMemo, useRef)`, `@react-three/fiber (useFrame — imported, unused)`, `three (THREE.CanvasTexture, THREE.Color, THREE.RepeatWrapping)`
**Data flow:** Pure mock. makeBlueprintTex (lines 10-35) and makeSchematicTex (lines 38-65) draw canvas textures. Schematic labels hardcoded: ['ANALYSIS SCHEMA','ENTITY MAP V2','QUERY PLAN'][seed%3] (line 63). schematicIdx passed as prop from parent to vary the three tables (0,1,2). No API/network.
**Styling:** 3D meshStandardMaterial colors and an emissive blueprint poster. Palette: blueprint floor #12284a with blue grid lines, gold trim #a8842c, archival walls #e8ecf4, ceiling #dce0e8.

**Details:** makeBlueprintTex: 512x512 canvas, fine grid every 32px (rgba(70,130,200,0.22)), major grid every 128px (rgba(100,170,235,0.55)), axis number labels '250'/'500'/'750' at i*128 (line 30), texture set to RepeatWrapping with repeat.set(3,6) (line 33) so it tiles across the 9.8x24 floor. makeSchematicTex(seed): 256x180 cream (#f0ead8) paper, 16px grid, draws shapes.slice(0, 2+seed) rectangles with crosshair center lines — note seed grows the number of shapes (idx 2 draws all 3 shapes; an idx>=2 would slice beyond array harmlessly). DraftingTable tilts surface and paper with rotation x=-0.3 (lines 78,83), gold legs, cross brace, and a per-table pointLight 'task lamp' (line 103, intensity 0.8, distance 3.5). TrackLighting: FIXTURES=5, rail box RW*0.9 wide, fixtures spaced via i/(FIXTURES-1) across RW*0.8, each with emissive bulb (emissiveIntensity 3) + pointLight (0.55, distance 5). DocumentRack: vertical frame, 4 shelves, document stacks count 3+(i%2) per shelf, alternating cream colors. Floor border loop (lines 179-184) maps a 2-element array but both entries are identical placeholders ([0,RW,0.14]) and only `i` is used for positioning (front/back gold strips at RZ-RD/2 + i*RD) — the array values are dead. Lighting: ambient #dce8f8 0.55, central warm pointLight 1.4 (distance 18), blue fill #4080c0 0.4. Three drafting tables at varied positions/rotations (lines 214-216); two track rails (z RZ+5, RZ-3); two doc racks on left wall.

**Issues:**
- _low_ (line 1-2): useRef and useFrame are imported but never used — dead imports (lint should flag). StudioFloor is fully static with no animation.
- _low_ (line 179-184): Floor-border loop maps over `[[0,RW,0.14],[0,RW,0.14]]` but never reads the tuple values — only index i is used. The array contents are dead; intent unclear (looks like leftover from a refactor).
- _low_ (line 56): shapes.slice(0, 2 + seed): for any DraftingTable with schematicIdx>2 this would request more than the 3 available shapes (slice clamps, so no crash, but the visual stops varying). Magic coupling between seed and shape count is fragile.
